[[Chuck Liddell]]
[[Tito Ortiz]]
[[Rich Franklin]] (as of the final episode)\n|country = United States\n|network = [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]]\n|first_aired = {{start date|2010|3|31}}{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=30705 |title=Liddell and Ortiz to coach TUF season 11 |publisher=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC.com]] |date=December 5, 2009 |access-date=December 6, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091212082109/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=30705 |archive-date=December 12, 2009 }}\n|last_aired =\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs. Team Ortiz''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs. Team Franklin''''' for the final episode of the season) is the eleventh installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. The show began taping in early 2010{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16650/the-ultimate-fighter-11-tryouts-draw-bobby-southworth-nick-thompson-logan-clark.mma |title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 11\" tryouts draw Bobby Southworth, Nick Thompson, Logan Clark |first=Dann |last=Stupp |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=October 28, 2009 |access-date=November 25, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121015105527/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16650/the-ultimate-fighter-11-tryouts-draw-bobby-southworth-nick-thompson-logan-clark.mma |archive-date=October 15, 2012 }} and premiered on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]] on March 31, 2010. The live finale of the show was June 19, 2010.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16349/the-ultimate-fighter-11-open-tryouts-set-for-oct-26-in-los-angeles.mma |title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 11\" open tryouts set for Oct. 26 in Los Angeles |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=September 30, 2009 |access-date=November 25, 2009 }}\n\nThe UFC and Spike TV held open tryouts on October 26, 2009, in [[Los Angeles]], [[California]]. The casting call went out for [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] and [[Light Heavyweight (MMA)|light heavyweight]] fighters. Fighters wanting to try out for the show must have been at least 21 years old and have had a professional MMA record. 300 fighters showed up to the tryouts including UFC veterans [[Jason Lambert]],{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=ys-mmaweektuf103109&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Veterans advance at TUF 11 tryouts |first=Steven |last=Marrocco |publisher=Yahoo Sports |date=October 31, 2009 |access-date=November 25, 2009}} [[Nick Thompson (fighter)|Nick Thompson]] and [[Logan Clark]], [[International Fight League]] and [[EliteXC]] veteran Wayne Cole, as well as season 1 ''TUF'' competitor and former [[Strikeforce (mixed martial arts)|Strikeforce]] light heavyweight champion [[Bobby Southworth]]. In addition to the open tryouts, the UFC also accepted applications available through its website until November 9, 2009.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16719/the-ultimate-fighter-11-application-deadline-set-for-nov-9.mma |title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 11\" application deadline set for Nov. 9 |date=November 3, 2009 |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |access-date=November 25, 2009 }}\n\nDuring [[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights Finale]], Dana White announced that the coaches would be [[Tito Ortiz]] and [[Chuck Liddell]]. White also announced that the fighters for this season would compete in the [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] weight class. The season will feature 28 fighters as opposed to the usual 16 or 32. The series boasts a new \"wildcard\" format in which the two coaches will pick two fighters who lost their preliminary bouts to face each other for the eighth spot in the quarter-finals.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17655/the-ultimate-fighter-11-to-feature-28-fighters-new-format-promised.mma |title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 11\" to feature 28 fighters, \"new format\" promised |first=Dann |last=Stupp |publisher=MMAJunkie |date=January 22, 2010 |access-date=January 24, 2010 }}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n*;{{color box|blue|border=silver}} '''Team Liddell'''\n**[[Chuck coach\n**[[Forrest Griffin]]\n**[[Tyson Griffin]]\n**[[Gray Maynard]]\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Ortiz'''\n:* [[Nick Ring]], [[Kyacey Uscola]], [[Kris McCray]], Jamie Yager, James Hammortree, Clayton McKinney, [[Chris Camozzi]] ([[Seth Baczynski]])*\n\n*'''Team Liddell'''\n:* [[Kyle Noke]], [[Rich Attonito]], Charles Blanchard, Josh Bryant, [[Brad Tavares]], [[Court McGee]], Joseph Henle\n\n''* Camozzi was replaced by Baczynski due to injury before official competition started on national television''\n\n*'''Fighters eliminated before entry round'''\n:* Brent Cooper, Jacen Flynn, Charley Lynch, [[Victor O'Donnell]], [[Norman Paraisy]], [[Costas Philippou]], Greg Rebello, [[Jordan Smith (fighter)|Jordan Smith]], Ben Stark, Lyle Steffens, Warren Thompson, Cleburn Walker, [[Woody Weatherby]]\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1: Smashed Up'''\n*Dana White welcomed 28 fighters to the \"new and improved\" UFC training facility and announced that only 14 would get into the Ultimate Fighter house.\n*He also announced a format change for the season: following the first round, White and the coaches would pick two fighters who were eliminated in the first round to be wild cards. Those two wild card fighters would fight for another chance to go to the second round.\n*The 28 fighters then fought to determine who would go to the Ultimate Fighter house. Elimination round matches were for two rounds. If there was a draw after two rounds, a third round would be fought.\n*'''Jamie Yager''' defeated Ben Stark by KO (head kick) at 0:26 of the first round.\n*'''Brad Tavares''' defeated Jordan Smith by KO (strikes) at 0:37 of the first round.\n*'''Kris McCray''' defeated Cleburn Walker by TKO (shoulder injury) at 0:15 of the first round.\n*'''James Hammortree''' defeated Norman Paraisy by TKO (retirement) at the end of the first round.\n*'''Nick Ring''' defeated Woody Weatherby by TKO (punches) at 2:42 of the first round.\n*'''Kyle Noke''' defeated Warren Thompson by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*'''Court McGee''' defeated Seth Baczynski by unanimous decision (29–28, 29–28, 29–28) after three rounds.\n*'''Chris Camozzi''' defeated Victor O'Donnell by unanimous decision (29–28, 29–28, 29–28) after three rounds.\n*'''Kyacey Uscola''' defeated Brent Cooper by KO (punches) at 0:25 of the first round.\n*'''Joseph Henle''' defeated Costas Philippou by submission (armbar) at 2:17 of the second round.\n*'''Rich Attonito''' defeated Lyle Steffens by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*'''Josh Bryant''' defeated Greg Rebello by majority decision (20–18, 19–19, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*'''Charles Blanchard''' defeated Jacen Flynn by TKO (referee stoppage) at the end of the first round.\n*'''Clayton McKinney''' defeated Charley Lynch by TKO (punches) at 4:07 of first round.\n\n;'''Episode 2: Suck it Up'''\n*Chuck Liddell and Tito Ortiz picked teams. White flips a coin (blue for Liddell, red for Ortiz) to decide who got the first pick. Ortiz got to pick the first fighter, while Liddell got to pick the first fight.\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n|-\n!Ortiz\n|Nick Ring\n|Kyacey Uscola\n|Kris McCray\n|Jamie Yager\n|James Hammortree\n|Clayton McKinney\n|Chris Camozzi\n|-\n!Liddell\n|Kyle Noke\n|Rich Attonito\n|Charles Blanchard\n|Josh Bryant\n|Brad Tavares\n|Court McGee\n|Joseph Henle\n|}\n*White confronted Liddell about his team, believing that his picks were much weaker than Ortiz's due to a size and height difference between the two teams. He revealed that he and Ortiz often have different assessments about fighters.\n*Team Liddell chooses Kyle Noke to fight Clayton McKinney.\n*Tension heated up between Jamie Yager and McKinney as Yager, Brad Tavares and Kris McCray used blowhorns at 3:00 a.m. and woke the other fighters.\n*McKinney thought his shoulder injury was a torn rotator cuff, leading Ortiz to believe McKinney was mentally weak and makes a point of pushing him harder. After an MRI, the doctor wrongly diagnoses the injury as just a bone contusion, therefore painful, but not serious.\n*'''Kyle Noke''' defeated Clayton McKinney by submission (triangle choke) at 2:44 of the first round.\n*A visibly frustrated McKinney walked away in disappointment, but was called back by Ortiz to immediately practice escaping triangle chokes. Attempting to boost the morale of McKinney and his team-mates, Ortiz reminded him that he could still win a wildcard slot.\n\n;'''Episode 3: A Lotta Heart'''\n*White called out Chris Camozzi telling him that what he thought was just a minor tooth ache, was actually a broken jaw. Because of this injury, Camozzi was sent home and Seth Baczynski replaced him.\n*Team Liddell chose Brad Tavares to fight James Hammortree.\n*Tensions boiled between Jamie Yager and Nick Ring during the training session with Yager claiming that the Canadian was coming on strong with kicks. He then called Ring a bitch, angering him.\n*'''Brad Tavares''' defeated James Hammortree by unanimous decision after three rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 4: Clown Box'''\n*Nick Ring confronted Yager for cheering for his friend Brad Tavares during his fight with James Hammortree, despite Tavares being a member of the opposing team.\n*Team Liddell chose Attonito to fight Uscola.\n*Tensions rose when Uscola accused Yager of stealing his clothes.\n*'''Rich Attonito''' defeated Kyacey Uscola by disqualification (illegal knee) at 3:36 of the first round.\n*After a dominating first few minutes by Attonito, Uscola began to come back, but then appeared to land an illegal knee to the head of a grounded Attonito. A flattened out Attonito appeared to be unable to continue and was ruled the winner by disqualification.\n*Immediately following the match debate ensued regarding the illegal knee, as post-fight replays and the interpretations of witnessing fighters and trainers resulted in the creation of a controversial decision.\n*White admitted that there was controversy surrounding the decision, but stated that he felt the referee had made the correct decision. *After the fight, a frustrated Ortiz punched a door off of its hinges on his way out of the gym.\n*Attonito returned to the house and broke the news that his hand was injured and may be broken.\n*Team Liddell chose Blanchard to fight Yager.\n*'''Jamie Yager''' defeated Charles Blanchard by TKO (strikes) at 1:09 of the first round.\n*Team Ortiz gains control of the matchups.\n\n;'''Episode 5: If It Breathes, It Bleeds'''\n*Wildcard predictions began by the fighters and James Hammortree stated his case, because of his \"controversial\" decision loss to Brad Tavares.\n*It was revealed that the \"bruised bone\" Clayton McKinney was diagnosed with was actually a ripped off labrum ligament. While describing the surgery to his housemates, McKinney tells them that the doctor had to scrape the humeral bone, put screws in it, shave the ligament and then anchor the labrum to the humeral by using the screws. Since this type of injury takes 3–6 months to heal completely, it eliminated McKinney from the wildcard race, along with Charles Blanchard who claimed that he is in no condition to beat anybody right now.\n*Ortiz made an apology to McKinney for making remarks about him not giving 100% during the earlier team training. McKinney accepted his apology but, as Ortiz walks away, gave him the middle finger.\n*Team Ortiz chose McCray to fight Bryant.\n*'''Josh Bryant''' defeated Kris McCray by unanimous decision after three rounds.\n*Team Liddell regained control of the matchups.\n\n;'''Episode 6: Did Your Dizzle'''\n*Kyacey Uscola expressed concern that Ortiz's training methods were \"too rough,\" believing they caused Kris McCray's loss. His teammates, including McCray, thought otherwise.\n*Team Liddell swept Team Ortiz in dodgeball to win the Coaches' Challenge.\n*The blue team chose McGee to fight Ring.\n*James Hammortree was sent to the hospital after being thrown by McCray and later revealed it may be a slipped disk.\n*'''Nick Ring''' defeated Court McGee by majority decision after two rounds.\n*Team Ortiz regains control of the matchups.\n*Liddell disagreed with the decision and showed anger and disdain towards White and other people in attendance.\n\n;'''Episode 7: Coming for Blood'''\n*Nick Ring expressed concern about a possible re-occurrence of a 3-year-old knee injury.\n*'''Seth Baczynski''' defeated Joseph Henle via unanimous decision after three rounds.\n*The wildcards were announced as Kyacey Uscola and Kris McCray.\n*As Rich Attonito had to withdraw from the competition, his spot was open. White gave the slot to Court McGee, without having to fight for it.\n*This upset Henle and James Hammortree who both believed they did enough in their fights to earn either a wildcard or Attonito's slot.\n\n;'''Episode 8: Closed Mouth Don't Get Fed'''\n*'''Kris McCray''' defeated Kyacey Uscola via submission (keylock) at 0:59 of the second round.\n*The quarter final matchups were announced as:\n:*Nick Ring vs. Court McGee\n:*Kyle Noke vs. Kris McCray\n:*Brad Tavares vs. Seth Baczynski\n:*Jamie Yager vs. Josh Bryant\n*Ring discovers that he required his third ACL reconstruction surgery and faced the choice between fighting on and risking tearing his meniscus, or withdrawing from the competition.\n*Ring withdrew and was replaced by Hammortree after he ran after White as he was leaving the house, stating he wanted the fight.\n*'''Court McGee''' defeated James Hammortree via submission (standing guillotine choke) at 0:28 of the second round.\n\n;'''Episode 9: Civilized Sport'''\n*Ortiz reveals that his doctors want him to have neck surgery immediately, as he is now regularly experiencing numbness in his limbs.\n*'''Kris McCray''' defeated Kyle Noke via unanimous decision after three rounds.\n*'''Brad Tavares''' defeated Seth Baczynski via disqualification (illegal soccer kick) at the end of the first round.\n*Baczynski had soccer kicked Tavares in the head as the buzzer went signalling the end of the first round. The Team Liddell member then faceplanted, leading Baczynski to apologize immediately. Tavares then asked \"for what?\" clearly not knowing what had just happened.\n*After consulting with the doctors, referee Herb Dean ended the fight, declaring Tavares the winner via disqualification, which marked the second time this season that such an event occurred.\n*The decision sparks a near-riot after Ortiz and Saul Soliz claim that the soccer kick caught Tavares in the armpit. Soliz claims that Team Liddell already got one bad decision go their way, this angered Rich Attonito who called out Soliz for disrespecting him.\n*After arguing with Joseph Henle about it, Ortiz proceeded to march towards him, leading Liddell to stand up for his fighter before being restrained by his team . It was later revealed by the replay that the kick landed solidly on the head.\n*Liddell was pulled aside by White who informed him that Ortiz had pulled out of their intended fight at [[UFC 115]]. The episode ends with Liddell walking out of the room saying \"I'm going to punch him.\"\n\n;'''Episode 10: Shocked and Awed'''\n*'''Josh Bryant''' defeated Jamie Yager via TKO (retirement) at the end of the second round.\n*After two rounds, the judges declared the fight a draw and the fight was to go to a third round. Yager complained about not being able to see and left the octagon in an ambulance with his neck stabilized as a precaution.\n*No substantive injuries were found in the hospital examination, however, and he was discharged in a few hours.\n*Ortiz is informed that he is being replaced as a coach. He at first leaves without explaining to the red team, leading White to have to inform them of his withdrawal. Ortiz then explains to his team a couple of hours later at the house.\n*Liddell is upset at being denied his rematch with Ortiz and swears angrily.\n*The semi-final matchups are announced as:\n:*Brad Tavares vs. Court McGee\n:*Kris McCray vs. Josh Bryant\n*Rich Franklin, former UFC middleweight champion, replaced Ortiz as the red team's head coach.\n\n;'''Episode 11: A Will to Win'''\n''For this episode, the starting credits changed to reflect Ortiz' departure and Rich Franklin's entrance. The season was renamed \"Team Liddell vs. Team Franklin.\" This was also a two-hour episode.''\n*Forrest Griffin, Tyson Griffin and Gray Maynard also come in as the assistant coaches.{{Cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=82330 |title=The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs Team Ortiz – Episode Ten Recap |publisher=UFC.com |access-date=2010-06-03}}\n*A number of fighters within the house decided to play a prank on Brad Tavares, Jamie Yager, Kris McCray and Kyle Noke in order to get revenge on a previous incident in which Tavares, Yager and McCray set off air horns in the middle of the night. This prank follows a similar structure to the original, in that it involves air horns during the early hours of the morning. This angers Tavares, who vows to get revenge.\n*'''Court McGee''' defeated Brad Tavares via technical submission (rear naked choke) at 4:45 of the third round.\n*White called the fighters to the gym where he introduces them to several members of the US Marines. The fighters spent the day training with the Marines before going back to the house and having a barbecue.\n*Tavares and Yager made up a concoction of flour and beans which they began to throw out of their window at some of their housemates who were sunbathing. This eventually caused a heated confrontation between Yager and James Hammortree, followed by another confrontation between Yager and Kyacey Uscola. Yager insulted his teammate's fighting credentials and Uscola offered to fight him outside, to which Yager agreed. However, as Uscola stands outside waiting for Yager and calling him out through his window, his teammate simply shuts his window and remains in his room.\n*'''Kris McCray''' defeated Josh Bryant via unanimous decision after three rounds.\n*After the fight, Franklin jokes that he is the only undefeated coach in the history of TUF.\n*McGee and McCray are announced as the season's finalists. Special mention was made of McCray setting the new record for fights per season, having fought five times (\"literally every Tuesday\" according to White).\n\n==Tournament Championship]] (UFC) at the [[Palms Casino Resort]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]], on June 19, 2010.{{cite web|url= http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=3233|title= The Ultimate Fighter: vs. Team Ortiz'' in the [[Middleweight (MMA)|Middleweight]] division.\n\n===Background===\n[[Kenny Florian]] filled in for [[Joe Rogan]] as color commentator, due to scheduling conflicts for the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on May 8, 2010, at the [[Bell Centre]] in [[Montreal]], [[Quebec]], [[Canada]].\n\n==Background==\nUFC 113 featured the rematch between [[Lyoto Machida]] and [[Maurício Rua]] for the [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]]. The two first met at [[UFC 104]], where Lyoto Machida retained his belt in a controversial unanimous decision victory. It also featured the official [[pay-per-view]] and introduction of Kimbo Slice into the heavyweight division of the UFC.\n\nFor the co-main event, a bout between former Light Heavyweight champions [[Rashad Evans]] and [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] was initially linked to this event, but was subsequently moved to [[UFC 114]] where the pairing served as the [[Headliner (performances)|headliner]].{{cite news |url=http://mmafrenzy.com/12649/rashad-evans-vs-quinton-rampage-jackson-likely-for-ufc-113-in-may/ |title=Rashad Evans vs. Quinton \"Rampage\" Jackson Likely for What looked to be a gesture of good sportsmanship turned out to be a [[sucker punch]] delivered by Daley which Koscheck blocked. In the post-fight press conference, Koscheck went on to say, \"Oh yeah, it hurt. It was the best shot he landed all night.\" Dana White stated \"He [Daley] will never fight in the UFC again.\"{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19061/dana-white-following-cheap-shot-ufc-113s-paul-daley-wont-fight-in-the-ufc-again.mma |title=Dana White: Following cheap shot, UFC 113's Paul Daley won't fight in the UFC again |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 9, 2010 |access-date=May 16, 2010 |archive-date=May 25, 2012 |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525021130/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19061/dana-white-following-cheap-shot-ufc-113s-paul-daley-wont-fight-in-the-ufc-again.mma |url-status=dead }}\n\nKimbo Slice, only 1-1 in UFC fights at the time, was to be subsequently released from the UFC despite losing in only his second fight for the promotion. In the post fight press conference, Dana White stated the bout was \"probably Kimbo's last fight in the UFC.\" He still praised Slice and admitted he made it farther in the promotion than he thought, stating \"He's impressed me as a human being and as a fighter, and I like him and the guys who represent him very much. I'm glad to have met Kimbo Slice.\"{{Cite web |last=Shalik |first=Michael |date=2010-05-10 |title=Dana White Explains Why Kimbo Slice Was Cut From The UFC 2]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 112: Invincible''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on April 10, 2010, in [[Abu Dhabi]], [[United Arab Emirates]].{{cite news|url= http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2890|title= UFC 112|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=January 27, 2010}} Due to the time zone difference in Abu Dhabi, UFC 112 was shown live on [[pay-per-view]] in the United States at 1 pm ET (10 am PT), with a replay at the regular time of 10 pm ET (7 pm PT).\n\nThe event was the first held in the Middle East following the purchase of a 10 percent stake in the UFC parent company [[Zuffa]] by [[Abu Dhabi]] government-owned Flash Entertainment. It was also the first UFC event to be held outdoors in an open-air arena.{{cite news|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/photos/concert-arena-photos/|title=Concert Arena Photos|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=April 9, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100409215907/http://www.mmafighting.com/photos/concert-arena-photos/|archive-date=April 9, 2010}} The Concert Arena (at the Ferrari World theme park) was erected as a temporary venue for the event and was torn down the following week.\n\n==Background==\n[[Vitor Belfort]] was expected to fight [[Anderson Silva]] for the [[UFC Middleweight Championship|Middleweight Championship]]; however, Belfort injured his shoulder during training and was unable to fight at the event.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17916/shoulder-injury-forces-vitor-belfort-out-of-ufc-112-title-fight-with-anderson-silva.mma|title= Shoulder injury forces Vitor Belfort out of UFC 112 title fight with Anderson Silva|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 11, 2010}} It was thought that Silva would instead take on [[Chael Sonnen]], but he was unable to take the short notice fight due to lacerations obtained in a [[UFC 109]] bout with [[Nate Marquardt]]. Silva ended up fighting [[Demian Maia]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17937/anderson-silva-ed-soares-excited-about-old-school-matchup-in-new-ufc-112-title-fight.mma|title=Anderson Silva, Ed Soares excited about \"old school\" matchup in new UFC 112 title fight.|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=February 13, 2010|archive-date=May 7, 2010|access-date=April 14, 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100507112505/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17937/anderson-silva-ed-soares-excited-about-old-school-matchup-in-new-ufc-112-title-fight.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nA bout between [[Paul Taylor (fighter)|Paul Taylor]] and [[John Gunderson]] was cancelled on the day of the fight because of health issues that Taylor faced after [[cutting weight]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18660/opponents-trainer-ufc-112-card-loses-paul-taylor-due-to-medical-issues.mma|title=UFC 112: Invincible\" card loses Paul Taylor due to medical issues|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=April 10, 2010|archive-date=May 25, 2012|access-date=April 10, 2010|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525133323/http://mmajunkie.com/news/18660/opponents-trainer-ufc-112-card-loses-paul-taylor-due-to-medical-issues.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Results==\n\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Anderson Silva]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Demian Maia]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (50–45, 50–45, 49–46)\n|5\n|5:00\n|For the [[UFC Middleweight Championship]].\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Frankie Edgar]]\n|def.\n|[[B.J. Championship]] on May 29, 2010, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]]. The UFC Fan Expo took place at the [[Mandalay Bay Convention Center]].\n\n==Background==\n[[Spike TV]] once again broadcast two live preliminary bouts one hour before the PPV broadcast began. In addition, the three-part ''[[UFC Primetime]]'' series returned to [[Spike (TV network)|Spike TV]] in the build up for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18749/spike-tv-confirms-three-part-ufc-primetime-rampage-vs-evans-special-for-ufc-114|title=Spike TV confirms three part UFC Primetime: Rampage vs. Evans special for UFC 114|date=16 April 2010|publisher=MMAjunkie|access-date=16 April 2010|archive-date=15 October 2012|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121015133537/http://mmajunkie.com/news/18749/spike-tv-confirms-three-part-ufc-primetime-rampage-vs-evans-special-for-ufc-114|url-status=dead}}\n\nThe full fight card featured five [[The Ultimate Fighter|Ultimate Fighter]] season champions ([[Rashad Evans]], [[Michael Bisping]], [[Diego Sanchez]], [[Amir Sadollah]] and [[Efraín Escudero]]), making it just the second [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event (after [[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom Finale]] the previous year) to feature 5 TUF champions.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17881/ufc-114-takes-place-at-mgm-in-las-vegas-ufc-fan-expo-2010-at-mandalay-bay.mma|title=UFC 114 takes place at MGM in Las Vegas, UFC Fan Expo 2010 at Mandalay Bay|author=Dan Stupp|publisher=MMAjunkie|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120711143632/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17881/ufc-114-takes-place-at-mgm-in-las-vegas-ufc-fan-expo-2010-at-mandalay-bay.mma|archive-date=2012-07-11}} Another Ultimate Fighter winner, [[Forrest Griffin]], was also scheduled to compete at this event, but had to pull out due to injury.{{citation needed|date=August 2025}}\n\nA bout between Forrest Griffin and [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]] was scheduled for this event, however Griffin withdrew due to a shoulder injury. Griffin was replaced by [[Jason Brilz]].{{cite web|last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18983/jason-brilz-agrees-to-face-antonio-rogerio-nogueira-at-ufc-114.mma |title=Jason Brilz agrees to face Antonio Rogerio Nogueira at UFC 114 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 4, 2010 |access-date=May 16, 2010}}\n\n[[Melvin Guillard]] was scheduled to face [[Thiago Tavares]], but Tavares was forced off the card with an elbow injury.{{cite news|url= http://mmafrenzy.com/14422/thiago-tavares-out-of-ufc-114-matchup-with-melvin-guillard/|title= Thiago Tavares Out of UFC 114 Matchup with Melvin Guillard\n|publisher=mmafrenzy.com |access-date=May 11, 2010}} [[Waylon Lowe]] then stepped in as Tavares' replacement.{{citation needed|date=August 2025}}\n\n[[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] President [[Dana White]] confirmed that the winner between [[Rampage Jackson|Jackson]] vs. Evans would fight [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Mauricio Rua]] for the title in their next bout, but it never took place after Rashad Evans (who won the UFC 114 main event) was injured and ultimately replaced by [[Jon Jones]] for the title match at [[UFC 128]] in March 2011. Rua's manager had originally suggested he could see Rua's first title defense against [[Randy Couture]], though that bout was not likely to occur.{{cite web |author=carmichaeldave |url=http://khtk.cbslocal.com/2010/05/10/dana-white-shoguns-first-defense-at-205/#more-606 |title=Dana White: Shogun's first defense at 205 « Sports 1140 KHTK |publisher=Khtk.cbslocal.com |date=May 10, 2010 |access-date=May 16, 2010 |archive-date=May 13, 2010 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100513064040/http://khtk.cbslocal.com/2010/05/10/dana-white-shoguns-first-defense-at-205/#more-606 |url-status=dead }}\n\nJackson was originally supposed to fight Evans for the title after defeating [[Keith Jardine]] at [[UFC 96]] in March 2009, but a jaw injury during that fight allowed [[Lyoto Machida]] to replace him in the [[UFC 98]] title fight, which Machida won. Jackson was then expected to fight for the title against Machida, but instead became a coach on [[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights]] against Evans.\n\n==Results==\n\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent This event was the UFC's debut on [[Versus (TV channel)|Versus]].{{cite news|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Vera-Jones-Agreed-for-Versus-Event-21597|title= Jones-Vera Agreed for Versus Event|publisher=sherdog.com|access-date=2009-12-16}} The event was also shown on various international networks including [[Rogers Sportsnet]] in [[Canada]], [[Televisa]] in [[Mexico]], and [[ESPN (UK)|ESPN]] in the [[United Kingdom|UK]].[http://mmajunkie.com/news/18234/ufc-announced-international-broadcast-plans-for-ufc-on-versus-1-vera-vs-jones.mma UFC announces international broadcast plans for \"UFC on Versus 1: Vera vs. Jones\"] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100312052713/http://mmajunkie.com/news/18234/ufc-announced-international-broadcast-plans-for-ufc-on-versus-1-vera-vs-jones.mma |date=2010-03-12 }}\n\n==Background==\n[[Brandon Vera]] was previously scheduled to fight [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]] at [[UFC 109]], but Nogueira was forced out of the bout with an ankle injury.{{cite news|url= http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Nogueira-Out-of-UFC-109-21573|title= Nogueira Out of UFC 109|publisher=sherdog.com |access-date=2009-12-15}}\n\nA bout between [[Clay Guida]] and [[Sean Sherk]] was in the works but Sherk suffered an undisclosed injury and was forced to pull out.{{cite news|url=https://www.sbnation.com/2010/1/15/1252957/Guida-Sherk-Fight-Scratched|title= Sean Sherk vs. Clay Guida scratched from UFC on Versus| publisher=sbnation.com|access-date=2010-01-15}} [[Shannon Gugerty]] stepped in as the replacement against Guida.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17573/sherk-scratched-gugerty-steps-in-to-face-guida-at-ufc-on-versus-1-in-march.mma|title=Sherk scratched; vs. Evans]]\n|followingevent=[[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs. Team Ortiz Finale]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 115: Liddell vs. Franklin''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on June 12, 2010, at [[General Motors Place]] (now Rogers Arena) in [[Vancouver, British Columbia]], [[Canada]].{{cite news|url= http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2958|title= UFC 115|publisher=ufc.com |access-date=2010-04-01}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Image:Chuck Liddell vs. Rich Franklin UFC 115.jpg|left|thumb|250px|Rich Franklin against Chuck Liddell in the main event of ''UFC 115'']]\nThis was the fourth UFC event to be held in Canada, following ''[[UFC 83]]'', ''[[UFC 97]]'' and ''[[UFC 113]]'' and the first UFC event held in British Columbia since they passed a bill to sanction MMA, on December 17, 2009.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2009/12/17/vancouver-to-regulate-mma-for-two-year-testing-period|title=Vancouver to Regulate MMA for Two-Year Testing Period|author=Mike Chiappetta|date=17 December 2009 }} For a few days, this event location was in jeopardy because there were still no regulations in place to promote the sport professionally within Vancouver{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2010/3/3/1334681/report-ufc-115-from-vancouver|title=Report: UFC 115 from Vancouver could be in jeopardy|author=Jesse Holland|date=3 March 2010 }} and [[Cincinnati]] was rumoured to be a possible location for moving this event.{{cite web |url=http://i.tsn.ca/mma/story/?id=315955 |title=Source: UFC 115 will not happen in Vancouver |publisher=I.tsn.ca |access-date=2011-03-02 |archive-date=2011-07-06 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110706205815/http://i.tsn.ca/mma/story/?id=315955 |url-status=dead }} However, the problems were resolved and [[Dana White]] confirmed that the show will be going ahead in Vancouver on the scheduled date.{{cite web|url=https://www.tsn.ca/mma/story/?id=316190 |title=UFC sorts out differences with Vancouver, UFC 115 is a go |publisher=Tsn.ca |access-date=2011-03-02}}\n\nThe main event was tentatively set to be a third bout featuring [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs. Team Ortiz|Ultimate Fighter coaches]] and former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|Light Heavyweight Champions]] [[Chuck Liddell]] and [[Tito Ortiz]]. In March, rumours began to circulate that [[Rich Franklin]] would replace Ortiz as Liddell's opponent, after Ortiz had supposedly withdrawn from the fight. However, Dana White denied this via his Twitter account on March 13, 2010, noting that Liddell vs. Ortiz was still set to be at the main event. Then, after the announcement, Ortiz had to withdraw from the match due to neck surgery.\n\nDespite confirming Liddell/Ortiz III would in fact be at the main event for the card, the main event was confirmed on April 12 as Liddell vs. Franklin due to a neck injury suffered by Ortiz.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2958 |title=Ultimate Fighting Championship |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2011-03-02}}\n\nThe on-again, off-again rematch between [[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]] and [[Jon Fitch]], first scheduled for ''[[UFC 107]]'' then ''[[UFC 111]]'' and then ''UFC 115'' was moved again, to ''[[UFC 117]]''.{{cite news |url=http://mmafrenzy.com/14294/thiago-alves-jon-fitch-rematch-pushed-back-to-ufc-117-in-august/|title=Thiago Alves-Jon Fitch Rematch Pushed Back to UFC 117 in August\n |publisher=mmafrenzy.com |access-date=2010-04-30}}\n\nThe [[Ben Rothwell]] vs. [[Gilbert Yvel]] fight replaced [[Tyson Griffin]] vs. [[Evan Dunham]] on the main card. Griffin vs. Dunham was shown on the preliminaries aired on Spike TV.\n\nJust like ''[[UFC 111]]'' in March, ''UFC 115'' was shown in movie theaters around the United States by [[National CineMedia|NCM Fathom]].\n\n==Results==\n\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Rich Franklin]]\n|def.\n|[[Chuck Liddell]]\n|KO (punch)\n|1\n|4:55\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on March 31, 2010 at [[Bojangles' Coliseum]] in [[Charlotte, North Carolina]],{{cite the third time the UFC held an event in Charlotte, but the first since [[UFC 5]], also at the same venue.\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the third UFC event in under two weeks along with [[UFC LIVE: Vera vs. Jones]] and [[UFC 111]]. The event also served as the lead-in program for [[The Ultimate Fighter 11]].\n\n[[Cole Miller]] was scheduled to face [[Andre Winner]], but was forced from the card with an injury and replaced by [[Rafaello Oliveira]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18114/rafaello-oliveira-replaces-cole-miller-re-completes-ufc-fight-night-21-card.mma|title=Rafaello Oliveira replaces Cole Miller, 2010|url-status=dead|archiveurl=https://web.archive.org/web/20100402065638/http://mmajunkie.com/news/18114/rafaello-oliveira-replaces-cole-miller-re-completes-ufc-fight-night-21-card.mma|archivedate=April 2, 2010}}\n\nA brief [[Power outage|blackout]] occurred in the [[Bojangles' Coliseum]] when [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]] vs. [[Stefan Struve]] was being announced.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/371731-ufc-fight-night-suffers-power-outage-roy-nelson-lights-it-back-up|title=UFC Fight Night Suffers Power Outage, Roy Nelson Lights It Back [[UFC Live: Jones vs. Matyushenko]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 116: Lesnar vs. Carwin''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on July 3, 2010, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.home&eid=3262|title=UFC 116: LESNAR vs. CARWIN|publisher=ufc.com |date=May 4, 2010}} The show was voted as the Best Major Show in the 2010 [[List of Wrestling Observer Newsletter awards|Wrestling Observer Newsletter Awards]], the first and only time an [[mixed martial arts]] and UFC show won the award.\n\n==Background==\n[[Shane Carwin]], who won the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|Interim Heavyweight Championship]] by defeating [[Frank Mir]] at [[UFC 111]], faced current heavyweight champion [[Brock Lesnar]]. The two were originally set to face each other at [[UFC 106]], then [[UFC 108]], before Carwin won the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|interim heavyweight championship]], but Lesnar pulled out of the fight both times due to an illness ([[diverticulitis]]) that kept him from training.\n\n[[Cheick Kongo]] was scheduled to face [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]],{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11341&zoneid=13 |title=ROY NELSON VS CHEICK KONGO AT UFC 116- MMA WEEKLY – Mixed Martial Arts & UFC News, Photos, Rankings & more |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=May 16, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100510233151/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11341&zoneid=13 |archive-date=May 10, 2010 }} but a back injury sidelined Kongo.{{cite web|url=http://riddum.com/index.php?option=com_content&view=article&id=716:cheick-kongo-injured-no-confirmation-on-roy-nelson-matchup-nor-ufc-116-return&catid=50:ufc-news&Itemid=122 |title=Exclusive – Cheick Kongo: injured, no confirmation on Roy Nelson matchup nor UFC 116 return |publisher=Riddum.com |date=April 30, 2010 |access-date=May 16, 2010}} Nelson fought [[Junior dos Santos]] at [[UFC 117]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19070/roy-nelson-vs-junior-dos-santos-in-the-works-for-ufc-117-main-card.mma |title=Roy Nelson vs. Junior Dos Santos in the works for UFC 117 main card |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 10, 2010 |access-date=May 16, 2010}}\n\n[[Alessio Sakara]] was set to fight [[Nate Marquardt]],{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11345&zoneid=13 |title=NATE MARQUARDT VS. ALESSIO SAKARA AT UFC 116- MMA WEEKLY – Mixed Martial Arts & UFC News, Photos, Rankings & more |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=May 16, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100510232728/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11345&zoneid=13 |archive-date=May 10, 2010 }} but Sakara pulled out of the bout after the death of his father.{{cite web|last=Holland |first=Jesse |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2010/5/2/1454804/report-alessio-sakara-withdraws |title=Report: Alessio Sakara withdraws from UFC 116 fight with Nate Marquardt |date=2 May 2010 |publisher=MMA Mania |access-date=May 16, 2010}}\n\n[[Jacob Volkmann]] was scheduled to fight [[Paul Kelly (mixed martial arts fighter)|Paul Kelly]] as one of the preliminary fights, but Kelly reportedly had to pull out of the July 3 fight due to visa issues.{{cite web|url=https://twitter.com/JacobVolkmann/status/15221435412 |title=Twitter / Jacob Volkmann: I will not be fighting July 3rd in Vegas because the stupid brit did not get his visa in time. I will kill him Aug 3rd. |publisher=Twitter.com |access-date=June 13, 2010}} The bout was rescheduled for [[UFC Live: Jones vs. Matyushenko]] the next month.\n\nAlso, UFC newcomer [[Ricardo Romero (fighter)|Ricardo Romero]] was scheduled to fight [[Steve Cantwell]] after Cantwell's recent medical ban ended. However, the fight was cancelled for unknown reasons{{cite web|url=http://www.heavy.com/mma/mma-news/2010/06/ricardo-romero-vs-steve-cantwell-in-the-works-for-ufc-116/|title=Ricardo Romero vs. Steve Cantwell In The Works For UFC 116|publisher=heavy.com |date=June 3, 2010}} and Cantwell was replaced by UFC returnee [[Seth Petruzelli]].{{cite web |last=Marrocco |first=Steven |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19415/seth-petruzelli-rejoins-ufc-meets-ricardo-romero-on-ufc-116-preliminary-card.mma |title=Seth Petruzelli rejoins UFC, meets Ricardo Romero on UFC 116 preliminary card |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=June 4, 2010 |access-date=June 13, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100608135647/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19415/seth-petruzelli-rejoins-ufc-meets-ricardo-romero-on-ufc-116-preliminary-card.mma |archive-date=June 8, 2010 }}\n\nOn June 17, 2010, Julio Paulino was injured and withdrew from his fight against [[Daniel Roberts (fighter)|Daniel Roberts]]. [[Forrest Petz]] returned to the UFC and replaced Paulino.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19576/forrest-petz-replaces-injured-julio-paulino-meets-daniel-roberts-at-ufc-116.mma|title=Forrest Petz replaces injured Julio Paulino, meets Daniel Roberts at UFC 116|publisher=MMAjunkie|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100619051540/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19576/forrest-petz-replaces-injured-julio-paulino-meets-daniel-roberts-at-ufc-116.mma|archive-date=2010-06-19}}\n\nOn June 22, 2010, [[Wanderlei Silva]] had to withdraw from his co-main event fight against [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]] due to three broken ribs and an injured right knee. Akiyama was then matched against [[Chris Leben]].{{Cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2010/06/22/wanderlei-silva-off-ufc-116|title=Wanderlei Silva Off UFC 116|author=Ariel Helwani|date=22 June 2010|publisher=MMA Fighting}}\n\nThe bout between [[Kendall Grove]] and [[Goran Reljic]] was originally slated for the live prelims show on Spike TV. However, it was later demoted and replaced by Seth Petruzelli vs. Ricardo Romero because of Grove's criticism of the Spike TV network and ''The Ultimate Fighter'' television show.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19689/grove-vs-reljic-demoted-petruzelli-vs-romero-promoted-to-ufc-116s-ufc-prelims.mma|title=Grove vs. Reljic demoted, Petruzelli vs. Romero promoted to UFC 116's August 7, 2010, at the [[Oracle Arena]] in [[Oakland, California]], United States.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC117#/fight|title=UFC 117: Silva vs. Sonnen|publisher=ufc.com |date=August 7, 2010}}\n\n==Background==\nUFC President [[Dana White]] announced via [[ESPN]] on [[Jim Rome is Burning]] that Middleweight Champion [[Anderson Silva]] would face [[Chael Sonnen]] on August 7.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18719/anderson-silva-vs-chael-sonnen-targeted-for-august-in-utah-potentially-ufc-117.mma|title=Anderson Silva vs. Chael Sonnen targeted for August in Utah, potentially UFC 117|publisher=mmajunkie.com |date=April 14, 2010}}{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/White-UFC-Planning-Silva-Sonnen-Showdown-23878|title=UFC Planning Silva-Sonnen Showdown|publisher=sherdog.com |date=April 14, 2010}}\n\nWhite threatened to cut Silva from the UFC if he fought the same way he did in his previous bout in Abu Dhabi at [[UFC 112]]. \"I'm telling you right now, if he acts like that again in the ring, I will cut him,\" said White. \"I don't care if he's the pound for pound best fighter in the world. I don't care if he's the middleweight champion.\"\n\nA bout between [[Joey Beltran]] and [[Matt Mitrione]] was reported to be in the works.{{cite web |url=http://truefighting.webs.com/apps/blog/show/3707205-beltran-vs-mitrione-in-the-works-for-ufc-117-in-oakland |title=TrueFighting.com |publisher=Truefighting.webs.com |date=May 10, 2010 |access-date=August 1, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100514221838/http://truefighting.webs.com/apps/blog/show/3707205-beltran-vs-mitrione-in-the-works-for-ufc-117-in-oakland |archive-date=May 14, 2010 }} The fight was later moved to [[UFC 119]].{{cite web |last=Marrocco |first=Steven |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19340/ufc-119-targeted-for-sept-25-in-indianapolis-mitrione-vs-beltran-on-tap.mma |title=UFC 119 targeted for Sept. 25 in Indianapolis, Mitrione vs. Beltran on tap |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 28, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120714063253/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19340/ufc-119-targeted-for-sept-25-in-indianapolis-mitrione-vs-beltran-on-tap.mma |archive-date=July 14, 2012 }}\n\n[[Stanislav Nedkov]] was scheduled to make his UFC debut against [[Rodney Wallace (fighter)|Rodney Wallace]], but was replaced by [[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]] after sustaining an injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20018/phil-davis-replaces-injured-stanislav-nedkov-meets-rodney-wallace-at-ufc-117.mma|title=Phil Davis replaces injured Stanislav Nedkov, meets Rodney Wallace at UFC 117|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=July 22, 2012}}\n\n[[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]] was scheduled to face [[Tim Boetsch]], but was forced off the card with an aggravated back injury.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20065/thiago-silva-injured-again-replacement-search-underway-for-ufc-117-slot.mma |title=Thiago Silva injured again, replacement search underway for UFC 117 slot |work=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=July 27, 2010 |last1=Morgan |first1=John |last2=Marrocco |first2=Steven |access-date=July 1, 2011 |url-status=dead Silva's replacement was Todd Brown.{{cite web |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20068/ufc-newcomer-todd-brown-replaces-thiago-silva-at-ufc-117-meets-tim-boetsch.mma |title=UFC newcomer Todd Brown replaces Thiago Silva at UFC 117, meets Tim Boetsch |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=July 27, 2010 |url-status=dead }}\n\nDana White confirmed that the winner of the [[Jon_Fitch|Fitch]] vs. [[Thiago_Alves_(fighter)|Alves]] fight would receive a title shot.{{cite web|last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19807/lesnar-vs-velasquez-up-next-for-ufc-title-dos-santos-vs-nelson-winner-on-deck.mma |title=Lesnar vs. Velasquez up next for UFC title; Dos Santos vs. Nelson winner vs. Sonnen]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC Fight Night: Marquardt vs. Palhares]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 118: Edgar vs. Penn 2''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on August 28, 2010, at the [[TD Garden]] in [[Boston]], [[Massachusetts]], [[United States]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC118#/fight|title=UFC 118: Edgar vs. Penn 2|publisher=ufc.com |date=June 17, 2010}} The third UFC Fan Expo coincided with the event.{{Cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=mmajunkie-UFC_Fan_Expo_UFC_118_Boston|title=UFC's third-ever UFC Fan Expo to coincide with August's UFC 118 in Boston|publisher=Yahoo!}} This event was the first the UFC held in Massachusetts.\n\n==Background==\n[[Nate Marquardt]] and [[Alessio Sakara]] were originally set to face each other at [[UFC 116]], but the fight was cancelled due to the death of Sakara's father.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20029/jorge-rivera-pulls-out-of-118-with-a-broken-arm.mma|title=Jorge Rivera pulls out of 118 with a broken arm|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=July 23, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120712073543/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20029/jorge-rivera-pulls-out-of-118-with-a-broken-arm.mma|archive-date=July 12, 2012}} On July 23, Sakara's original opponent, [[Jorge Rivera (fighter)|Jorge Rivera]] suffered a broken arm in training and was replaced by [[Gerald Harris (fighter)|Gerald Harris]]. On August 5, Sakara (who was back on the card) then bowed out due to an unknown injury and was replaced by [[Joe Vedepo]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20184/joe-vedepo-replaces-alessio-sakara-faces-gerald-harris-at-ufc-118.mma|title=Joe Vedepo replaces Alessio Sakara, faces Gerald Harris at UFC 118|publisher=MMAjunkie|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100817201559/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20184/joe-vedepo-replaces-alessio-sakara-faces-gerald-harris-at-ufc-118.mma|archive-date=2010-08-17}} On August 18, it was announced that the Harris/Vedepo bout had been scrapped altogether.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20334/ufc-118-fight-card-complete-with-10-bous-harris-vs-vedepo-off-prelims.mma|title=UFC 118 fight card complete with 10 bous; Harris vs. Vedepo off preliminary card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=August 18, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100820121833/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20334/ufc-118-fight-card-complete-with-10-bous-harris-vs-vedepo-off-prelims.mma|archive-date=August 20, 2010}}\n\nA bout between [[Nate Marquardt]] and [[Rousimar Palhares]] was moved to [[UFC Fight Night 22]] and promoted to the new main event. The original Fight Night main event was scheduled to be [[Demian Maia]] vs. [[Alan Belcher]], however, Belcher had to withdraw due to an injured retina.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20141/nate-marquardt-vs-rousimar-palhares-moved-to-ufc-fight-night-22-main-event-slot.mma|title=Nate Marquardt vs. Rousimar Palhares|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=August 2, 2010}} As a result of this, Maia was moved to this card and fought Mario Miranda.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20150/demian-maia-vs-mario-miranda-on-tap-for-ufc-118-in-august.mma|title=Demian Maia vs. Mario Miranda on tap for UFC 118 in August|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=August 3, 2010}}\n\nThe event featured the first MMA appearance of a professional boxer with three-time world champion [[James Toney]]. The debut of a professional boxer took place at [[UFC 1]] when [[Art Jimmerson]] lost to [[Royce Gracie]] by submission.\n\nOn August 5, it was announced that [[Terry Etim]] had suffered a broken rib in training and was pulling out of his bout with [[Joe Lauzon]]. Etim was replaced by former [[UFC 63|UFC]] and [[The Ultimate Fighter 5|TUF]] veteran [[Gabe Ruediger]].{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20185/tuf-5-vet-gabe-ruediger-replaces-terry-etim-faces-joe-lauzon-at-ufc-118.mma|title=\"TUF 5\" vet Gabe Ruediger replaces Terry Etim, faces Joe Lauzon at UFC 118|publisher=MMAjunkie|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100817201605/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20185/tuf-5-vet-gabe-ruediger-replaces-terry-etim-faces-joe-lauzon-at-ufc-118.mma|archive-date=2010-08-17}}\n\n[[Phil Baroni]] was scheduled to face [[John Salter]], but Baroni was forced off the card on July 27 with a collarbone injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20067/phil-baroni-off-ufc-118-card-due-to-collarbone-injury.mma|title=Phil Baroni off UFC 118 card due to collarbone injury|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=July 27, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100730015337/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20067/phil-baroni-off-ufc-118-card-due-to-collarbone-injury.mma|archive-date=July 30, 2010}} [[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] served as Baroni's replacement.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=12156&zoneid=13 |title=- DAN MILLER REPLACES BARONI AT UFC 118- MMA WEEKLY - Mixed Martial Arts & UFC News, Photos, Rankings & more |access-date=2010-07-29 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100729165927/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=12156&zoneid=13 |archive-date=2010-07-29 }}\n\nOn August 25, Dana White announced that the winner of the [[Kenny Florian|Florian]] vs. [[Gray Maynard|Maynard]] fight would be guaranteed the next shot at the lightweight title.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20422/ufc-118s-kenny-florian-vs-gray-maynard-winner-guaranteed-lightweight-title-shot.mma|title=UFC 118's Kenny Florian vs. Gray Maynard winner guaranteed lightweight title shot|publisher=MMAjunkie|access-date=2010-08-26|archive-date=2012-05-25|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525132635/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20422/ufc-118s-kenny-florian-vs-gray-maynard-winner-guaranteed-lightweight-title-shot.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Frankie Edgar]] (c)\n|def\n|[[B.J. Fighting Championship]] on September 25, 2010, at [[Conseco Fieldhouse]] in [[Indianapolis]], [[Indiana]], United States.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC119#/fight|title=UFC 119: Mir vs. Cro Cop|publisher=ufc.com |date=2010-07-19}} The event was the first that the UFC hosted in Indiana.\n\n==Background==\nOn August 15, 2010, [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]] had to pull out of his bout with [[Frank Mir]] due to an injury that would require surgery. It later was reported to be a recurring hip injury.[http://mmajunkie.com/news/20290/report-mirko-cro-cop-filipovic-replaces-big-nog-in-ufc-119-headliner.mma Report: Mirko \"Cro Cop\" Filipovic replaces \"Big Nog\" in UFC 119 headliner] 2010-08-16[http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=5077 CroCop steps in for injured Nog against Mir, UFC 119] bout was rescheduled and took place at [[UFC 140]] in December 2011.\n\n[[Aaron Riley]] was expected to face UFC newcomer Pat Audinwood at this event, but Riley was forced from the card with an injury and was replaced by [[Thiago Tavares]].[http://mmajunkie.com/news/20326/aaron-riley-out-thiago-tavares-in-against-pat-audinwood-at-ufc-119.mma Aaron Riley out, Thiago Tavares in against Pat Audinwood
