B. Dollaway'''\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n\n==See also==\n* [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n* [[List of UFC champions]]\n* [[List of UFC events]]\n* [[2010 in UFC]]\n\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n{{DEFAULTSORT:Ufc 119: Mir Vs. Crocop}}\n[[Category:Ultimate Fighting Championship flagship events]]\n[[Category:2010 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:Mixed martial arts in Indiana]]\n[[Category:Sports competitions in Indianapolis]]\n[[Category:2010 in Indiana]]\n[[Category:2010s in Indianapolis]]\n[[Category:Long stubs with short prose]]\n\n{{Mixedmartialart-stub}}"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC 119"}]},{"pageid":26425717,"ns":0,"title":"UFC 120","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2010}}\n{{Use dmy dates|date=April 2022}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 120: Bisping vs. Akiyama\n|image=UFC 120 Poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date= October 16, 2010\n|venue= [[The O2 Arena (London)|O2 Arena]]\n|city= [[London]], [[United Kingdom]]\n|attendance= 17,133\n|gate= $2,560,000\n|previousevent= [[UFC 119|UFC 119: Mir vs. Cro Cop]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC 121|UFC 121: Lesnar vs. Velasquez]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 120: Bisping vs. Akiyama''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on October 16, 2010, at the [[The O2 Arena (London)|O2 Arena]] in [[London]], [[United Kingdom]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC120_london_event#/fight |title=UFC 120: Bisping vs. Akiyama|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|date=July 20, 2010|access-date=July 20, 2012}} The card featured six televised MMA bouts, as well as four un-aired preliminary bouts.\n\nThe main event featured ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 3]]'' winner [[Michael Bisping]] and the Japanese judoka [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]]. After three rounds, the fight went to a judges decision, where Bisping was victorious with scores of 30–27 being given by all three ringside judges. The co-main event of the evening featured [[Dan Hardy]] (who entered the contest having become the first Englishman to compete in a UFC Championship bout, albeit unsuccessfully) and [[Carlos Condit]], the former [[List of WEC champions#Welterweight Championship|World Extreme Cagefighting Welterweight champion]]. Condit was able to [[knockout]] Hardy in the opening round; consigning Hardy to his first knockout loss.\n\nThe first UK [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] Fan Expo coincided with this event and was held at the [[Earls Court Exhibition Centre]] on Friday October 15 and Saturday October 16. Aside from the live broadcast in Britain and Canada, this event aired on the same day, via [[Broadcast delay|tape delay]], on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike TV]] in the U.S.\n\nThe event was the fourth the UFC has hosted at the [[The O2 Arena (London)|O2 Arena]] in [[London]], England, [[United Kingdom|U.K.]] and the first since [[UFC 95]]. It was also the ninth event held in United Kingdom and the first since [[UFC 105]]. UFC 120 was also notable for becoming, at that time, the ninth highest attended event in UFC history.\n\n==Background==\n\n===Main event===\n[[Image:MichaelBisping.png|right|thumb|130px|Michael Bisping attempted to maintain his perfect record in Europe and attempted to move closer to title contention]]\nThe main event featured ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 3]]'' tournament winner [[Michael Bisping]] facing off against the Japanese judoka practitioner [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]] in a fight contested at the UFC middleweight limit of 185 pounds (84 kg). Michael Bisping sought to defeat Akiyama in his home country in the hope of moving closer to the [[List of UFC champions#Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Championship]] picture.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21038/home-field-advantage-for-michael-bisping-ufc-120-headliner-not-so-sure.mma|title=Home field advantage for Michael Bisping; UFC 120 headliner not so sure|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 15, 2010|access-date=October 15, 2010|last=Stupp|first=Dann|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129115455/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2010/10/home-field-advantage-for-michael-bisping-ufc-120-headliner-not-so-sure|archive-date=January 29, 2013}} Additionally, Bisping looked to maintain his perfect record in Europe, having only ever been defeated in the United States and Australia.\n\nBisping came into the fight with an MMA record of 19 wins and 3 losses, of which 9 wins and 3 losses were within the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21030/ufc-120-preview-can-new-coaches-change-trajectory-of-bisping-vs-akiyama.mma|title=UFC 120 preview: Can new coaches change trajectory of Bisping vs. Akiyama|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 16, 2010|access-date=October 16, 2010|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|archive-date=18 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101018024644/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21030/ufc-120-preview-can-new-coaches-change-trajectory-of-bisping-vs-akiyama.mma|url-status=dead}} Bisping had had early success in the promotion, following his win in ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 3]]'' – a reality television show produced by the UFC, which consisted of an 8-man tournament, in which Bisping was victorious in the [[Light Heavyweight (MMA)|light heavyweight division]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Michael-Bisping-Confirms-Split-with-Longtime-Gym-Wolfslair-40211|title=Michael Bisping confirms split with longtime gym Wolfslair|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=February 11, 2012|access-date=February 11, 2012|last=Whitman|first=Mike}} Bisping compiled a record of 4 wins and 1 loss as a light heavyweight, before he dropped to the middleweight division. From there, Bisping won 3 straight fights and was selected to be a head coach on the ninth season of ''The Ultimate Fighter'' ([[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom]]) alongside former [[PRIDE Fighting Championships]] champion [[Dan Henderson]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/pressreleases/THE-ULTIMATE-FIGHTER-UNITED-STATES-VS-UNITED-KINGDOM-PREMIERES-APRIL-1-16501|title=The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom premieres April 1|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=March 9, 2009|access-date=March 9, 2009}} His subsequent fight with Henderson began a slightly unsuccessful 2–2 streak for Bisping, as Henderson was able to defeat Bisping via knockout at [[UFC 100]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/UFC-100-Analysis-The-Main-Card-18506|title=UFC 100 Analysis: The Main Card|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=July 14, 2009|access-date=July 14, 2009|last=Leidecker|first=Tim}} Bisping followed this up with a comeback win over [[Denis Kang]] at [[UFC 105]] (the last English-based UFC event until UFC 120), having survived an early knockdown to eventually win via TKO.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Bisping-Win-Eradicates-Some-Damage-from-Henderson-Loss-20966|title=Bisping win eradicates some damage from Henderson loss|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=November 16, 2009|access-date=November 16, 2009|last=Savage|first=Greg}} Bisping would next face [[Wanderlei Silva]] at [[UFC 110]], where Bisping suffered a unanimous decision loss,{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Bisping-Wants-Another-Crack-at-Silva-24795|title=Bisping wants another crack at Silva|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=May 30, 2010|access-date=May 30, 2010|last=Hunt|first=Loretta}} but he came back at [[UFC 114]] against [[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] to claim a convincing decision victory of his own, meaning that Bisping was 2–2 in the last 12 months. Bisping then took the opportunity to improve his coaching staff prior to the bout.\n\n[[Image:Yoshihiro Akiyama.jpg|left|thumb|140px|Yoshihiro Akiyama was headlining his first UFC card and looked to rebound from a loss at UFC 116]]\nAkiyama came into the bout with an MMA record of 13 wins, 2 losses and 2 no-contests, of which one win and one loss took place within the Ultimate Fighting Championship. Akiyama was an incredibly popular{{Clarify|reason=vague|date=May 2014}} personality in his home nations of South Korea and Japan and whilst his signing wasn't particularly celebrated in the United States or England,{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/1/The-Man-with-Two-Souls-17420|title=The Man with Two Souls Two Souls|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=May 13, 2009|access-date=May 13, 2009|last=Loiseleur|first=Tony}} it was big news in the far east, where Akiyama is known not only for his fighting career, but also for his singing career and his status as a mild fashion icon, which led to the forming of his nickname \"Sexyama\".{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/2/The-Man-with-Two-Souls-17420|title=The Man with Two Souls Odd Compliment|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=May 13, 2009|access-date=May 13, 2009|last=Loiseleur|first=Tony}} Despite his popularity in the far east, Akiyama had not yet headlined a UFC event prior to UFC 120, having only competed twice in the United States.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Akiyama-Joins-Jackson-Camp-for-Bisping-Bout-at-UFC-120-26583|title=Akiyama joins Jackson camp for Bisping bout at UFC 120|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=August 27, 2010|access-date=August 27, 2010|last=Critchfield|first=Tristen}} His U.S. debut came at [[UFC 100]], in the opening bout on the [[UFC 100]] pay-per-view event against [[Alan Belcher]], where Akiyama won via a disputed split decision (30–27, 28–29, 29–28). Many pundits were vocal in their disagreement with Akiyama winning the bout,{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/akiyama-gets-controversial-win-over-belcher-2|title=- AKIYAMA GETS CONTROVERSIAL WIN OVER BELCHER |publisher=MMAWeekly.com|date=July 11, 2009|access-date=July 11, 2009|author=Staff}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/ufc/2012/9/2/3277784/alan-belcher-tells-why-he-trained-with-a-fractured-spine|title=Alan Belcher tells why he trained with a fractured spine|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=September 2, 2012|access-date=September 2, 2012|last=Doyle|first=Dave}} with even UFC president [[Dana White]] stating \"I wasn't 100 percent sure of the decision, you know what I mean, but I think it was a tough fight to score, too\".{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2009/07/12/did-the-judges-rob-alan-belcher-at-ufc-100-with-yoshihiro-akiyama-decison-win/|title=Did the judges rob Alan Belcher at UFC 100 with Yoshihiro Akiyama decision win?|publisher=MMAmania.com|date=July 12, 2009|access-date=July 12, 2009|author=Staff}} Akiyama's second UFC appearance (and final appearance prior to UFC 120) was the co-main event of [[UFC 116]] against [[Chris Leben]]. Unlike the Belcher fight, Akiyama was regarded as the aggressor throughout the bout and was most likely en route to another decision victory.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19805/ufc-116-fighter-bonuses-six-fighters-each-earn-75k-awards.mma|title=UFC 116 fighter bonuses: Six fighters each earn $75k awards|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=July 4, 2010|access-date=July 4, 2010|author=Staff|archive-date=3 April 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130403123540/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2010/07/ufc-116-fighter-bonuses-six-fighters-each-earn-75k-awards|url-status=dead}} However, with seconds left in the final round, Leben was able to secure a [[triangle choke]] submission to force Akiyama to tap-out. Despite the loss, the bout was regarded as highly entertaining and both Leben and Akiyama took away the \"Fight of the Night\" bonus, worth $75,000.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Bisping-I-Know-Im-Going-to-Win-27491|title=Bisping: \"I know I'm going to win\"|publisher=sherdog.com|date=October 15, 2010|access-date=October 15, 2010|last=Myers|first=Joe}} The fight was Akiyama's first loss in over five years and it led to Akiyama moving his training camp to [[Albuquerque, New Mexico]], under the tutelage of [[Greg Jackson (MMA trainer)|Greg Jackson]], the World MMA Awards \"Trainer of the Year\" in 2010{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2010/12/01/world-mma-awards-2010-winners|title=World MMA Awards 2010 winners|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=December 1, 2010|access-date=September 29, 2012|last=Tucker|first=Bryan}} and 2011.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26324/2011-world-mma-awards-ceremony-live-updates-1030-p-m-et.mma|title=2011 World MMA Awards ceremony: live updates|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=November 30, 2011|access-date=November 30, 2011|author=Staff|archive-date=3 December 2011|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20111203002101/http://mmajunkie.com/news/26324/2011-world-mma-awards-ceremony-live-updates-1030-p-m-et.mma|url-status=dead}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/8/23/3263470/greg-jackson-responds-dana-white-ufc-152-jon-jones-vs-chael-sonnen|title=Greg Jackson responds to Dana White's criticism, suggests Jon Jones vs Chael Sonnen for UFC 152|publisher=MMAmania.com|date=August 23, 2012|access-date=August 23, 2012|last=Holland|first=Jesse}}\n\nPrior to the main event, both fighters seemed confident due to their new trainers. Akiyama was quoted as saying \"I felt like he didn't have enough time to prepare for my opponent [Leben] and so during the fight I wasn't able to come up with a strategy that is specifically for Chris Leben.\" Akiyama also mentioned \"I was able to learn the intricate skills of the takedowns, as well as better striking for MMA. I feel like [I have] a better takedown than Bisping does.\" Bisping responded by stating: \"With respect, I couldn't care less who he trains with. He can train with anyone. What I'm focusing on these days is what I do, focusing on my training. And he can worry about what I'm bringing to the table. My training's going fantastic. I feel in great shape. I've improved all my areas. Regardless of who he's training with, I think I'll be too much for him. Now everything's coming together. Mario Sukata, he's always been my MMA coach. But I've been working with some new striking coaches, strength and conditioning coaches, new boxing coaches, a new wrestling coach.\"\n\n===Co-main event===\n\nThe co-main event featured recent [[UFC Welterweight Championship|UFC Welterweight title]] challenger [[Dan Hardy]] facing off against the former [[World Extreme Cagefighting]] welterweight champion [[Carlos Condit]] in a fight contested at the UFC welterweight limit of 170 pounds (77.5 kg). Dan Hardy was looking to return to winning ways having been defeated in his title fight back at [[UFC 111]], whereas Condit looked to improve after a difficult start to life in the UFC.\n\n[[Image:Dan Hardy UFC 105.jpg|right|thumb|225px|Dan Hardy aimed to recover from a title loss at UFC 111 by taking on former WEC champion, Carlos Condit]]\nHardy entered the fight with an MMA record of 23 wins, 7 losses with 1 no contest, of which 4 wins and 1 loss came in fights within the UFC.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20988/ufc-120s-carlos-condit-says-dan-hardy-made-a-big-miscalculation.mma|title=UFC 120s Carlos Condit says Dan Hardy made a big miscalculation|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 12, 2010|access-date=October 12, 2010|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|archive-date=15 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101015211413/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20988/ufc-120s-carlos-condit-says-dan-hardy-made-a-big-miscalculation.mma|url-status=dead}} Hardy had joined the UFC in late 2008, after compiling a record of 19–6, predominantly in the English MMA circuit. He quickly made a name for himself defeating MMA veteran [[Akihiro Gono]] via split decision at [[UFC 89]] and [[Rory Markham]] via KO at [[UFC 95]];{{cite web|title=UFC 95 notebook: Hardy baits Davis|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=February 23, 2009|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/UFC-95-Notebook-Hardy-Baits-Davis-16304|access-date=February 23, 2009|last=Knapp|first=Brian}} both taking place in England. Hardy then began to gain recognition as he openly baited [[Marcus Davis (fighter)|Marcus Davis]] into a fight at [[UFC 99]] by mocking Davis' Irish heritage by stating: \"He's had a couple of fights in the UK, and he's kind of building himself a fan base, but he's not English; he's not Irish. I was born here, and I've been bred here. I don't mind taking on that challenge and showing him this is my home and not his.\" Hardy went on to defeat Davis and was placed in a title contender fight against [[Mike Swick]] at [[UFC 105]], in Manchester, England.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Update-HardySwick-Title-Eliminator-Set-for-UFC-105-20019|title=Update: Hardy-Swick title eliminator set for UFC 105|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=September 28, 2009|access-date=September 28, 2009|author=Staff}} After defeating Swick, Hardy faced the champion [[Georges St-Pierre]] at [[UFC 111]]. This was Hardy's last fight before UFC 120 and he was thoroughly dominated by St-Pierre's wrestling and top control resulting in a loss via unanimous decision (50–43, 50–44, 50–45).{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/GSP-Shuts-Out-Hardy-Carwin-KOs-Mir-23505|title=GSP shuts out Hardy, Carwin KOs Mir|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=March 28, 2010|access-date=March 28, 2010|last=Knapp|first=Brian}}\n\n[[Image:Carloscondit.jpg|left|thumb|130px|Carlos Condit held a UFC record of 2–1, prior to UFC 120]]\nCondit entered the fight with an MMA record of 25 wins and 5 losses, of which 2 wins and 1 loss were in the UFC. Condit moved to the UFC in April 2009, having been the final [[List of WEC champions#Welterweight Championship|World Extreme Cagefighting Welterweight Champion]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/1/The-Weekly-Wrap-March-28-April-3-16854|title=The Weekly Wrap: March 28 – April 3|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=April 4, 2009|access-date=April 4, 2009|last=Encarnacao|first=Jack}} Condit had arguably struggled since his move to the UFC, however, as he began with a split decision loss to [[Martin Kampmann]] at [[UFC Fight Night 18]] and then followed it up with a split decision victory over [[Jake Ellenberger]] at [[UFC Fight Night 19]], which was disputed by some pundits{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16195/ufc-fight-night-19-live-play-by-play-and-official-results.mma|title=UFC Fight Night 19: Play-by-play and official results|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=September 16, 2009|access-date=September 16, 2009|author=Staff|archive-date=22 September 2009|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090922170609/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16195/ufc-fight-night-19-live-play-by-play-and-official-results.mma|url-status=dead}} and the live crowd in attendance.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16203/ufc-fight-night-19-main-card-results-diaz-submits-guillard-maynard-edges-huerta.mma|title=UFC Fight Night 19 main card results: Diaz submits Guillard, Maynard edges Huerta|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=September 16, 2009|access-date=September 16, 2009|last1=Morgan|last2=Stupp|first1=John|first2=Dann|archive-date=22 September 2009|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090922170633/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16203/ufc-fight-night-19-main-card-results-diaz-submits-guillard-maynard-edges-huerta.mma|url-status=dead}} Condit's final fight before UFC 120 was against the youngster [[Rory MacDonald (fighter)|Rory MacDonald]]. Condit was taken down 3 times in the opening round and largely controlled. Condit came back with a more aggressive attitude in the final round and finished MacDonald with a combination of elbows and punches. After the fight, the judges scorecards were revealed and showed that had MacDonald not been finished in the last seven seconds, then he would have picked up a split decision victory.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19514/ufc-115-main-card-recap-franklin-ices-liddell-in-first-cro-cop-chokes-barry.mma|title=UFC 115 main card recap: Franklin ices Liddell in first; Cro Cop chokes Barry|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=June 13, 2010|access-date=June 13, 2010|last=Morgan|first=John|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120710145532/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19514/ufc-115-main-card-recap-franklin-ices-liddell-in-first-cro-cop-chokes-barry.mma|archive-date=July 10, 2012}} Though Condit was 2–1 in the UFC, each fight had been close, which was in contrast to Hardy's largely successful start in the promotion.\n\nPre-fight hype for the co-main event focused on Hardy's well-documented desire to fight someone who could put on an entertaining fight. Hardy was vocal in his criticism of [[Nik Lentz]] after Lentz defeated Hardy's teammate, [[Andre Winner]] in a fight marred by a perceived lack of action.{{cite web|url=http://www.thisisnottingham.co.uk/Dan-Hardy-Column/story-12247150-detail/story.html|title=Dan Hardy column: Lentz went into hiding for the big fight|publisher=This is Nottingham|date=September 7, 2010|access-date=September 7, 2010|last=Hardy|first=Dan|archive-date=29 September 2012|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120929084945/http://www.thisisnottingham.co.uk/Dan-Hardy-Column/story-12247150-detail/story.html|url-status=dead}} Before his fight with Condit, Hardy said \"Rather than saying \"oh, these guys can't wrestle\", I think the problem is there's beginning to be too much wrestling in the UFC Octagon, not too little of it in the gym. There are a lot of people out there calling themselves \"UFC fighters\" who are nothing of the kind. In the UFC, you should go for finishes.\" The comments drew criticism by fighters and pundits who believed he was referring to his fight against [[Georges St-Pierre]],{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=5678378|title=Hardy making MMA entertaining again|publisher=ESPN|date=October 14, 2010|access-date=October 14, 2010|last=Teague|first=Hywel}} which Hardy later denied, stating: \"People are assuming I'm making these comments because of that fight and I'm not. [St-Pierre] had his submissions attempts, and he played the game the right way. I was under pressure in the fight – there was no point where he was just holding me down. But there are some fighters who think, 'I've got a takedown, I can rest a bit here now.' There just isn't the intention to damage the opponent or submit them.\" When Hardy was paired up with Condit (ironically a teammate of St-Pierre's), Hardy seemed happy with the match-up, saying \"When Joe Silva [the UFC matchmaker] called me up and said 'How about Condit?' I said to myself, 'That's an awesome fight.' I know he's going to come to have a fight, there's no doubt about it, he'll do everything he can to win. That's what I'm interested in.\"\n\n===Main card===\n\nThe third main card bout featured English fighter [[John Hathaway (English wrestler)|John Hathaway]] facing MMA veteran [[Mike Pyle (fighter)|Mike Pyle]] in the welterweight division. Hathaway was coming off an upset victory over ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 1]]'' winner, [[Diego Sanchez]]{{cite web|url=http://blogs.telegraph.co.uk/sport/garethadavies/100008800/ufc-114-huge-upset-as-brit-hathaway-schools-sanchez-in-las-vegas/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100531072405/http://blogs.telegraph.co.uk/sport/garethadavies/100008800/ufc-114-huge-upset-as-brit-hathaway-schools-sanchez-in-las-vegas/|url-status=dead|archive-date=May 31, 2010|title=UFC 114 Huge upset as Brit Hathaway schools Sanchez in Las Vegas|publisher=The Telegraph|date=May 30, 2010|access-date=May 30, 2010|last=Davies|first=Gareth A.}} Hathaway was regarded as England's top prospect due to his takedown defence being higher than most fighters in the country. Hathaway was also undefeated, holding an MMA record of 14 wins and 0 defeats, with 4 of those wins coming in UFC events.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20951/ufc-120s-john-hathaway-mike-pyle-is-a-step-up-from-diego-sanchez.mma|title=UFC 120s John Hathaway: \"Mike Pyle is a step up from Diego Sanchez\"|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 9, 2010|access-date=October 9, 2010|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|archive-date=11 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101011101351/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20951/ufc-120s-john-hathaway-mike-pyle-is-a-step-up-from-diego-sanchez.mma|url-status=dead}} Pyle was an experienced veteran, holding a professional MMA record of 20 wins, 7 losses and 1 draw and a UFC record of 2–2. His most recent fight prior to UFC 120 was a win over Jesse Lennox at [[UFC 115]] via triangle choke submission. Before the fight, Pyle sought to downplay the achievement of Hathaway defeating Diego Sanchez, stating: \"Diego's a small, somewhat average-(sized) [155-pound fighter].\" Hathaway responded by stating \"I definitely think he's [Pyle] a step up. [Pyle is] incredibly experienced and very dangerous. I think he finishes like 80 to 90 percent of his fights by submission, so he's definitely a dangerous opponent.\"\n\nAlso on the main card was a [[Heavyweight (MMA)|heavyweight]] (206–265 pounds) fight between French fighter and UFC veteran [[Cheick Kongo]] and the relative newcomer [[Travis Browne]]. Kongo held an MMA record of 15 wins, 6 losses and 1 draw, with a UFC record of 8–4. Browne, on the other hand was undefeated going into the fight and won in his UFC debut which took place 4 months prior to UFC 120. For Kongo, this bout was a chance for redemption. Kongo had lost two fights in mid/late 2009 and whilst he was victorious in his final bout prior to UFC 120, he had been accused of unsportsmanlike conduct by many fans, after being deducted a point (on the judges' scorecards) against [[Paul Buentello]] at [[UFC Live: Vera vs. Jones]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18451/despite-latest-incident-ufcs-cheick-kongo-insists-hes-not-a-dirty-fighter.mma|title=Despite latest incident, UFCs Cheick Kongo insists he's not a dirty fighter|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=March 25, 2010|access-date=March 25, 2010|last=Morgan|first=John|archive-date=28 March 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100328203818/http://mmajunkie.com/news/18451/despite-latest-incident-ufcs-cheick-kongo-insists-hes-not-a-dirty-fighter.mma|url-status=dead}} For Browne, it was a chance to build on his successful UFC debut, where he defeated [[James McSweeney]] via [[technical knockout|TKO]] in the opening round of their bout. Browne held a professional record of 10–0 coming into the fight and of his 10 victories, he had finished the fight in the first round due to [[knockout|KO]] or TKO in 7 of them.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19790/heavyweights-cheick-kongo-vs-travis-browne-targeted-for-ufc-120.mma|title=Heavyweights Cheick Kongo vs. Travis Browne targeted for UFC 120|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=July 2, 2010|access-date=July 2, 2010|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100705005932/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19790/heavyweights-cheick-kongo-vs-travis-browne-targeted-for-ufc-120.mma|archive-date=July 5, 2010}}\n\nThe opening fight on the main card was a welterweight contest between [[Claude Patrick]] and the winner of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom]]'', [[James Wilks]]. Prior to the fight, Wilks had an MMA record of 7 wins and 3 losses, with 2 wins and a loss coming within the UFC.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20889/ufc-120s-james-wilks-respects-ufcs-win-or-go-home-policy.mma|title=UFC 120s James Wilks respects UFCs win or go home policy|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 4, 2010|access-date=October 4, 2010|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|archive-date=7 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101007110902/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20889/ufc-120s-james-wilks-respects-ufcs-win-or-go-home-policy.mma|url-status=dead}} Patrick, who had an MMA record of 12 wins and 1 loss, was making his second UFC appearance, having won in his debut via [[guillotine choke]] just a few months earlier.\n\n===Injuries and replacements===\n\n[[Dan Hardy]] was originally scheduled to fight [[Dustin Hazelett]] at this event before Hazelett pulled out because of his wedding in September.{{cite web|url=http://www.fightersonlymag.com/content/news/12317-dan-hardy-vs-dustin-hazelett-is-off|title=Dan Hardy vs. Dustin Hazelett is off|publisher=Fighters Only|date=May 7, 2010|access-date=May 7, 2010}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} [[Carlos Condit]] replaced Hazelett as Dan Hardy's opponent. [[John Hathaway (English wrestler)|John Hathaway]] was also expected to fight the then-undefeated [[Dong Hyun Kim]]. However, Kim was forced off the card with an elbow injury sustained during training and was replaced by [[Mike Pyle (fighter)|Mike Pyle]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2010/07/20/mike-pyle-replaces-injured-kim-will-face-john-hathaway-at-ufc-1/|title=Mike Pyle Replaces Injured Dong Hyun Kim, Will Face John Hathaway at UFC 120|publisher=MMAFighting.com|last=Chiappetta|first=Mike|date=20 July 2010 |access-date=August 25, 2010}}\n\nTom Blackledge, an assistant coach for Team Rampage on ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights]]'' was originally set to fight [[James Te-Huna]]. However, Te-Huna withdrew after an injury in training.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmabay.co.uk/2010/07/11/exclusive-tom-blackledge-vs-james-mcsweeney-in-the-works-for-october-16th-show-in-london/|title=Tom Blackledge vs. James McSweeney in the works for October 16th show in London|publisher=MMABay.co.uk|date=July 11, 2010|access-date=July 11, 2010|last=Pepper|first=Michael}} Blackledge was then scheduled to fight TUF 10 semi-finalist, [[James McSweeney]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19899/james-mcsweeney-returns-to-205-pounds-meets-tom-blackledge-at-ufc-120.mma/|title=James McSweeney returns to 205 pounds, meets Tom Blackledge at UFC 120|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=July 11, 2010|access-date=July 11, 2010|author=Staff|archive-date=13 July 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100713205228/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19899/james-mcsweeney-returns-to-205-pounds-meets-tom-blackledge-at-ufc-120.mma|url-status=dead}} However, on September 28, 2010, Tom Blackledge withdrew from the matchup with James McSweeney for unknown reasons and was replaced by [[Fabio Maldonado]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Maldonado-Replaces-Blackledge-at-UFC-120-27191|title=Maldonado replaces Blackledge at UFC 120|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=September 28, 2010|access-date=September 28, 2010|last=Whitman|first=Mike}}\n\n[[Steve Cantwell]] was scheduled to fight [[Stanislav Nedkov]], however the fight was cancelled less than 48 hours prior to the event, after Cantwell suffered a knee injury in training.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21031/knee-injury-knocks-steve-cantwell-out-of-ufc-120-prelims.mma|title=Knee injury knocks Steve Cantwell out of UFC 120 prelims|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 15, 2010|access-date=October 15, 2010|author=Staff|archive-date=18 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101018034931/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21031/knee-injury-knocks-steve-cantwell-out-of-ufc-120-prelims.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Card criticism===\n\nUFC 120 had drawn criticism before it had even taken place. The most common theme was that pundits and fans felt that the UFC events held in England were too focused on English fighters, rather than the \"big name\", world recognised fighters that audiences in the United States were used to.{{cite web|url=http://www.fightofthenight.com/news/ufc-120-tickets-selling-well-despite-criticisms-over-the-line-up/|title=UFC 120 tickets selling well despite criticisms over the line-up|publisher=Fight of the Night|date=July 25, 2010|access-date=July 25, 2010}} Past UFC events held in England featured title fights, such as [[UFC 80]] (held in [[Newcastle upon Tyne|Newcastle, England]]), which had a [[UFC Lightweight Championship]] bout; the last time a UFC championship fight took place in England.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21015/zelaznik-ufc-title-fights-not-necessarily-impossible-for-future-european-cards.mma|title=Zelaznik: UFC title fights not necessarily impossible for future European cards|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 14, 2010|access-date=October 14, 2010|last=Morgan|first=John|archive-date=16 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101016065711/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21015/zelaznik-ufc-title-fights-not-necessarily-impossible-for-future-european-cards.mma|url-status=dead}} Other main events held in England featured well known former champions, such as [[Randy Couture]]{{cite web|url=http://mma-info.net/2010/07/ufc-120-card%E2%80%99s-criticism-responded-by-zelaznik/|title=UFC 120 card: Criticism responded to by Zelaznik|publisher=MMA Betting Tips|date=July 23, 2010|access-date=July 23, 2010|archive-date=31 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101031145807/http://mma-info.net/2010/07/ufc-120-card%E2%80%99s-criticism-responded-by-zelaznik/|url-status=dead}} and [[Mirko Filipović|Mirko \"Cro Cop\" Filipović]], though UFC 120 was perceived to have less star power. The complaints by English fans were slightly exacerbated due to the length of time between when events were held in the country. UFC had only held one event in England in 2010 (UFC 120), which was down from two events in 2009 and three in 2008.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21053/ufc-brass-british-mma-far-from-dead-local-talent-not-required-for-expansion.mma|title=UFC brass: British MMA far from dead; local talent not required for expansion|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 17, 2010|access-date=October 17, 2010|last1=Morgan|last2=Stupp|first1=John|first2=Dann|archive-date=18 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101018094520/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21053/ufc-brass-british-mma-far-from-dead-local-talent-not-required-for-expansion.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nThe criticism resulted in members of the UFC – both management and fighters – defending the event. The UK president of the UFC, Marshall Zelaznik stated: \"Obviously, championship fights for the most part are elevated to the [[pay-per-view]] cards. The pay-per-view cards over here, because of the time change, make it a bit of a challenge in the U.S. to see the impact you're hoping to see from the championship fights.\" In an effort to appease the dissatisfied fans, he also added: \"It's a bit of a business decision, but that's not to say we'll never have a pay-per-view here or we'll never have a championship fight here.\"\n\n[[John Hathaway (English wrestler)|John Hathaway]], who was competing on the main card at UFC 120 said: \"I think it's a good card. It's great to see so many British fighters fighting international opponents. I'm more than happy with the card, I'm happy to compete, and I'm always keen to put a good performance on for the fans.\"{{cite web|url=http://www.mmabay.co.uk/2010/09/04/ufc-120-%E2%80%93-john-hathaway-backs-london-card-despite-ongoing-criticism-from-the-fans/|title=UFC 120s John Hathaway backs London card despite ongoing criticism|publisher=MMABay.co.uk|date=September 4, 2010|access-date=September 4, 2010|last=Pepper|first=Michael}}\n\n[[Dana White]], the UFC president also defended the event, stating: \"You cannot say a card sucks until it's over. It's like when we are sitting down and debating over who is going to win a fight. The cards get criticised and those are the events that normally end up being the most exciting, with great fights on them. At the end of the day you are paying to see good fights as a fan. I guarantee the card we are bringing to England will be great.\"{{cite web|url=https://www.telegraph.co.uk/sport/othersports/boxing/7931867/Exclusive-UFC-president-Dana-White-promises-UK-fans-great-night-of-fights.html|title=Exclusive: UFC president Dana White promises UK fans a great night of fights|publisher=The Telegraph|date=August 7, 2010|access-date=August 7, 2010|last=Davies|first=Gareth A.}}\n\n===UFC Fan Expo===\n\nThe UFC also held their first international Fan Expo, having held their first expo at [[UFC 100]].{{cite web |url=http://az290933.vo.msecnd.net/ufcfanexpo.com/RNA/RNA_UFCFanExpo_V2/documents/2011/UFC-Fan-Expo-London-Announcement.pdfx$query$xepslanguagex$eq$xenx$and$xvx$eq$x634270073577066985?enc=gzip |title=UFC® FAN EXPO™ HITS LONDON FIRST-EVER INTERNATIONAL FAN EXPO TO BE HELD AT EARLS COURT OCTOBER 15–16, 2010 |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship |date=July 30, 2010 |access-date=September 29, 2012 }}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} It was held at [[Earls Court Exhibition Centre]] on October 15 and 16 and it allowed fans to visit over 100 MMA-related vendors and also get autographs from several UFC fighters who attended the expo. Around two dozen fighters were announced for the event to sign autographs and interact with fans,{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/fan-expo-london-attendees-release|title=Fighters At London Fan Expo|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|date=September 16, 2010|access-date=September 16, 2010}} with other fighters, such as Randy Couture and [[Wanderlei Silva]] holding their own seminars.{{cite web|url=http://az290854.vo.msecnd.net/ufcfanexpo.com/RNA/RNA_UFCFanExpo_V2/documents/2011/UFCLO_OnsitePR_.pdfx$query$xepslanguagex$eq$xenx$and$xvx$eq$x634270073461907787?enc=gzip|title=LAST CHANCE TO PURCHASE DISCOUNTED TICKETS TO UFC® FAN EXPO™ LONDON 2010|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|date=October 14, 2010|access-date=October 14, 2010}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}\n\n===Attendance and gate figures===\n\nDespite the negative reaction to the event and its participants, Marshall Zelaznik announced that UFC 120 was expected to break the European attendance and gate receipts records, having sold out the original seating plan.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/pressreleases/UFC-120-BISPING-v-AKIYAMA-IS-SOLD-OUT-27361|title=UFC 120: Bisping vs. Akiyama is sold out!|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=October 7, 2010|access-date=October 7, 2010}} The gate record prior was 16,693, set by [[UFC 105]], with the record gate receipts coming in at $2.7m at [[UFC 75]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20924/executive-ufc-120-expected-to-set-european-attendance-and-gate-records.mma|title=Executive: UFC 120 expected to set European attendance and gate records|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 7, 2010|access-date=October 7, 2010|author=Staff|archive-date=9 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101009205702/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20924/executive-ufc-120-expected-to-set-european-attendance-and-gate-records.mma|url-status=dead}} This later proved partially correct, as the event set a new European attendance record of 17,133, but fell just short of the gate receipts figure with $2.56m.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21047/ufc-120-reportedly-sets-european-attendance-record.mma|title=UFC 120 reportedly sets European attendance record with 17,133 at The O2|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 16, 2010|access-date=October 16, 2010|last=Morgan|first=John|archive-date=13 July 2012|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120713074302/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21047/ufc-120-reportedly-sets-european-attendance-record.mma|url-status=dead}} With over 17,000 spectators, the event became, at that time, the ninth most attended event in UFC history.\n\nDespite the record attendance, the viewing figures on Spike TV were reduced from the last event to take place in England. UFC 120 averaged 1.9 million viewers, whereas UFC 105, in November 2009 had averaged 2.9 million.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21072/ufc-120-ratings-spike-tv-broadcast-averages-1-9-million-viewers-for-london-show.mma|title=UFC 120 ratings: Spike TV broadcast averages 1.9 million viewers (Updated)|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=October 19, 2010|access-date=October 19, 2010|author=Staff|archive-date=21 October 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101021005000/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21072/ufc-120-ratings-spike-tv-broadcast-averages-1-9-million-viewers-for-london-show.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Results==\n\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent [[Anaheim, California]], [[United States]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC121#/fight|title=UFC 121: Lesnar vs. Velasquez|publisher=ufc.com|date=August 16, 2010}} The event was the fourth time the UFC hosted at the [[Honda Center]] (formerly [[Arrowhead Pond]]) in [[Anaheim, California]] following [[UFC 59]], [[UFC 63]], and [[UFC 76]], and the sixth event held in the [[Greater Los Angeles Area]], including [[UFC 60]] and [[UFC 104]].\n\n==Background==\nJust like [[UFC 111]], [[UFC 115]], and [[UFC 118]], UFC 121 was shown in movie theaters around the United States by [[National CineMedia|NCM Fathom]].{{Cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/UFC-121-Hits-Big-Screens-Nationwide|title = UFC 121 Hits Big Screens Nationwide | UFC|date = 14 September 2018}} UFC 121 featured the return of preliminary fights live on Spike TV.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/McGee-Back-On-Spike|title=Spike TV to Telecast \"UFC 121 Prelims\" Featuring Court McGee vs. Ryan Jensen & Patrick Cote vs. Tom Lawlor on Saturday, October 23 |publisher=ufc.com|date=August 19, 2010}} ''[[UFC Primetime]]'' also returned to promote the [[Brock Lesnar]] vs. [[Cain Velasquez]] title fight.{{Cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/8/25/1650475/via-sportsnation-chat-w-dana|title = UFC Primetime: Lesnar vs. Velasquez in the works. This Fan Shot was promoted to the front page|date = 25 August 2010}}\n\n[[Jon Madsen]] was expected to face [[Todd Duffee]], but Duffee was forced off the card due to a lingering knee injury and was replaced by [[Gilbert Yvel]].{{cite web |url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2010/aug/24/gilbert-yvel-meet-jon-madsen-ufc-121/|title=Gilbert Yvel, Jon Madsen to meet at UFC 121 |date=24 August 2010 |access-date=2010-08-24|publisher=lasvegassun.com}} The event was notable for the post-fight confrontation between [[Professional wrestling|professional wrestler]] [[The Undertaker]] and Brock Lesnar following Lesnar's loss to Cain Velasquez.{{Cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2010/10/24/wwes-undertaker-brock-lesnar-exchange-words-following-ufc-121|title=WWE's Undertaker Confronts Brock Miller vs Tibau bout.\n}}\n{{MMAevent end|notes=yes}}\n\n==Controversy==\nMarquardt defeated [[Rousimar Palhares]] via TKO (punches) at 3:28 of round 1 at the main-event of UFC Fight Night 22. During the fight Palhares went for a heel-hook however Marquardt slipped his leg free and quickly turned the tables for a first-round TKO victory. However, this came with some controversy. Once Marquardt pulled his leg free, Palhares looked at his hand and signalled to officials and seemingly complained that Marquardt was greased. Marquardt capitalized on the opening and pounded Palhares with a quick barrage of punches. The ending, though, wasn't due to any illegal circumstances. UFC broadcaster [[Joe Rogan]] immediately announced that Marquardt had passed an inspection from the ringside physician and the referee. Marquardt explained that before the fight; \"I came out really warm because I wanted a good sweat,\" which explains why he was slippery and managed to remain elusive.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20661/ufc-fight-night-22-recap-slippery-marquardt-tkos-palhares-in-headliner.mma|title=UFC Fight Night 22 recap: Slippery Marquardt TKOs Palhares in headliner|publisher=MMAjunkie|access-date=2011-01-18|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100919153422/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20661/ufc-fight-night-22-recap-slippery-marquardt-tkos-palhares-in-headliner.mma|archive-date=2010-09-19|url-status=dead}}{{Cite $489,685\n|previousevent= [[UFC 116|UFC 116: Lesnar vs. Carwin]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC 117|UFC 117: Silva vs. Sonnen]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Live: Jones vs. Matyushenko''''' (also known as '''''UFC on Versus 2''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on Sunday, August 1, 2010, at the [[San Diego Sports Arena]] in [[San Diego]], California.{{cite web|url=http://uk.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.home&eid=3378|title=UFC Live: Jones VS. Matyushenko|publisher=uk.ufc.com|date=2010-05-24|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100528164943/http://uk.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=3378|archive-date=2010-05-28}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was initially scheduled to take place at the [[EnergySolutions Arena]] in [[Salt Lake City, Utah]].{{cite web|url=https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/4/20/1431911/august-features-ufc-on-versus-2-in|title=August features UFC on Versus 2 in Utah|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Scott Haber|date=2010-04-20|access-date=2019-02-27|archive-date=April 12, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220412011231/https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/4/20/1431911/august-features-ufc-on-versus-2-in|url-status=dead}} On June 14, 2010, the promotion decided to move the event to a different venue and location due to low ticket sales.{{cite web|url=http://lasvegassun.com/news/2010/jun/14/ufc-moves-event-salt-lake-city-san-diego-due-lack-/|title=UFC moves event from Salt Lake City to San Diego for lack of ticket sales|publisher=lasvegassun.com|author=Brett Okamoto|date=2010-06-14|access-date=2016-06-09}} The event was the second UFC event to be broadcast on [[Versus (TV channel)|Versus]]; the first being [[UFC Live: Vera vs. Jones]].\n\n[[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]] and [[Gleison Tibau]] were set to fight at this event, but the bout was later moved to [[UFC Fight Night: Marquardt vs. Palhares]] the following month.{{cite web |url=https://mmajunkie.com/2010/07/miller-vs-tibau-pearson-vs-miller-set-for-ufc-fight-night-22-main-card|title=Jim Miller vs. Gleison Tibau set for UFC Fight Night 22 main card |publisher=mmajunkie.com |date=2010-07-12}}\n\nTwo bouts were moved to this event due to visa issues; [[Paul Kelly (fighter)|Paul Kelly]] vs. [[Jacob Volkmann]] from [[UFC 116]]{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19392/paul-kelly-vs-jacob-volkmann-moved-from-ufc-116-to-ufc-on-versus-2.mma |title=Paul Kelly vs. Jacob Volkmann moved from UFC 116 to UFC on Versus 2 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2010-06-02 |access-date=2010-08-02 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100804210128/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19392/paul-kelly-vs-jacob-volkmann-moved-from-ufc-116-to-ufc-on-versus-2.mma |archive-date=2010-08-04 }} and [[Darren Elkins]] vs. [[Charles Oliveira]] from [[TUF 11 Finale|The Ultimate Fighter 11 Finale]].{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19430/gunderson-vs-holst-official-for-tuf-11-finale-elkins-vs-oliveira-to-ufc-on-versus-2.mma |title=Gunderson vs. Holst official for TUF 11 Finale, Elkins vs. Oliveira to UFC on Versus 2 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2010-06-05 |access-date=2010-08-02 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121015205809/http://mmajunkie.com/news/19430/gunderson-vs-holst-official-for-tuf-11-finale-elkins-vs-oliveira-to-ufc-on-versus-2.mma |archive-date=2012-10-15 }}\n\n[[Joe Stevenson]] suffered a knee injury in training and was unable to fight [[Takanori Gomi]]. Stevenson was replaced by [[Tyson Griffin]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=83511 |title=Griffin in, Stevenson out against Gomi on August 1st in San Diego |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2010-08-02 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100702223923/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=83511 |archive-date=2010-07-02 }}\n\n[[Willamy Freire]] also suffered an injury in training, forcing the cancellation of his fight with [[Thiago Tavares]]. Tavares was then pulled from the card altogether.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19994/injury-forces-willamy-freire-off-ufc-on-versus-2-thiago-tavares-without-opponent.mma|title=Injury forces Willamy Freire off UFC on Versus 2; Thiago Tavares without opponent|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=July 20, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120710183024/mmajunkie.com/news/19994/injury-forces-willamy-freire-off-ufc-on-versus-2-thiago-tavares-without-opponent.mma|archive-date=July 10, 2012}} .{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20031/with-no-opponent-available-thiago-tavares-pulled-from-ufc-on-versus-2.mma|title=With no opponent available, Thiago Tavares pulled from UFC on Versus 2 fight card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=July 23, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100725212106/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20031/with-no-opponent-available-thiago-tavares-pulled-from-ufc-on-versus-2.mma|archive-date=July 25, 2010}}\n\n[[Big John McCarthy|\"Big\" John McCarthy]] was a referee at this event, the first time he has refereed at a UFC event in almost 3 years.{{cite web|url=https://twitter.com/TheSavageTruth/status/18169146839 |title=Twitter / Greg Savage: Just heard \"Big\" John McCarthy |publisher=Twitter.com |access-date=2010-08-02}}\n\n[[Damarques Johnson]] originally weighed in at 172.5 pounds, 1.5 pounds over the contracted weight limit. After being granted one hour to make the original 171 pound limit, Johnson tipped the scales at 172 pounds. As a result, Johnson forfeited 20% of his purse for the fight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20115/ufc-on-versus-2-live-weigh-in-results.mma|title=UFC on Versus 2 live weigh-in results|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=August 1, The casting call went out for [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] and [[Light Heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]] fighters. All fighters applying and trying out for the show needed to have at least three professional fights and be at least 21 years of age.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18247/the-ultimate-fighter-12-open-tryouts-held-april-1-for-lightweights-light-heavyweights.mma|title=The Ultimate Fighter 12 |publisher= mmajunkie.com|date=March 10, 2010 |access-date=April 7, 2010 }} Some of the fighters that showed up for the auditions included former TUF contestant [[Jason Guida]] and [[PRIDE FC|Pride]] veteran [[Charles Bennett (fighter)|Charles Bennett]].{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/18548/the-ultimate-fighter-12-open-tryouts-draw-jason-guida-krazy-horse-mike-budnick.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 12\" open tryouts draw Jason Guida, \"Krazy Horse,\" Mike Budnik|publisher=mmajunkie.com}}\n\nIt was confirmed at the [[UFC 113]] Q&A that the winner of the [[Josh Koscheck]] and [[Paul Daley]] bout at [[UFC 113]] would coach against [[Georges St-Pierre]] and also fight for the [[UFC Welterweight Championship|Welterweight Championship]] that St-Pierre currently holds.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19042/georges-st-pierre-josh-koscheckpaul-daley-winner-to-coach-the-ultimate-fighter-12.mma|title=Georges St-Pierre, Josh Koscheck-Paul Daley winner to coach The Ultimate Fighter 12|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=August 13, 2010}} Koscheck eventually won the fight to coach opposite St-Pierre.\n\nOriginally this season was to have lightweights and light heavyweights, but it was confirmed that it will only have Lightweights.\n\nProduction began on June 7, 2010 and the season once again features 28 competitors, with the wildcard slot available, which debuted in the [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Liddell vs. Team Ortiz|eleventh season]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/19436/the-ultimate-fighter-12-goes-into-production-today-28-lightweights-chosen.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 12\" goes into production today, 28 lightweights chosen}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n\n;{{color box|red|border=silver}} Team GSP:\n* [[Georges St-Pierre]]\n* [[Greg Jackson (MMA trainer)|Greg Jackson]]\n* [[Phil Nurse]]{{Cite GSP|url=https://www.mmanews.com/2010/06/the-ultimate-fighter-12-list-of-assistant-coaches-for-kos-and-gsp/|access-date=2022-02-18|website=MMA News|language=en-US}}\n* [[Freddie Roach (boxing)|Freddie Lucio\n* [[Daniel Cormier]]\n\n===Fighters===\n* '''Team Koscheck'''\n:*Marc Stevens, Sevak Magakian, [[Sako Chivitchian]], Andy Main, [[Nam Phan]], Aaron Wilkinson, Jeff Lentz\n* '''Team GSP'''\n:*[[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]], [[Jonathan Brookins]], Spencer Paige, [[Alex Caceres]], Kyle Watson, [[Cody McKenzie]], Dane Sayers\n* '''Fighters eliminated before entry round'''\n:*[[J.J. Ambrose]], Paul Barrow, Jason Brenton, [[Mike Budnik]], [[Joseph Duffy (fighter)|Joseph Duffy]], [[Pablo Garza (fighter)|Pablo Garza]], Toby Grear, Daniel Head, [[Amir Khillah]], Steve Magdaleno, TJ O'Brien, [[Mike Richman]], Ariel Sexton, Ran Weathers\n\n==Episodes==\n'''Episode 1: Go For It'''\n* Dana White welcomed 28 fighters to the UFC training facility and announced that like the eleventh season, only 14 would get to go to the TUF house. He also announced the return of the wildcard format.\n* The 28 fighters then fought to determine who would go to the TUF house. Elimination round matches were for two rounds. If there was a draw after two rounds, a third round would be fought.\n* '''Marc Stevens''' defeated TJ O'Brien by KO (punches) at 0:13 of the first round.\n* '''Spencer Paige''' defeated Steve Magdaleno by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''Nam Phan''' defeated Mike Budnik by TKO (liver strike and punches) in the first round.\n* '''Andy Main''' defeated Jason Brenton by submission (triangle armbar) at 3:33 of the first round.\n* '''Jonathan Brookins''' defeated Ran Weathers by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''Sako Chivitchian''' defeated Toby Grear by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''Jeff Lentz''' defeated Daniel Head by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:45 of the second round.\n* '''[[Alex Caceres]]''' defeated Paul Barrow by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:55 of the first round.\n* '''Michael Johnson''' defeated Pablo Garza by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''Aaron Wilkinson''' defeated Mike Richman by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''[[Kyle Watson (fighter)|Kyle Watson]]''' defeated Joseph Duffy by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:33 of the first round.\n* '''Sevak Magakian''' defeated JJ Ambrose by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n* '''Cody McKenzie''' defeated Amir Khillah by technical submission (guillotine choke) at 2:15 of the first round.\n* '''Dane Sayers''' defeated Ariel Sexton by submission (standing rear naked choke) in the second round.\n\n'''Episode 2: Well Played'''\n* The fighters moved into the new house and immediately, Lentz' smoking and drinking came into focus, with some fighters questioning his cardio as a result.\n* The coin toss (red for GSP, yellow for Koscheck) was won by Josh Koscheck, who chose to pick the first fighter.\n* Georges St-Pierre hatched a plan to manipulate Koscheck into picking Marc Stevens as the top overall pick, so that his could make Johnson his own first pick. The plan seemingly worked as St-Pierre's wish came to fruition.\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n|-\n!Koscheck\n|Marc Stevens\n|Sevak Magakian\n|Sako Chivitchian\n|Andy Main\n|Nam Phan\n|Aaron Wilkinson\n|Jeff Lentz\n|-\n!GSP\n|Michael Johnson\n|Jonathan Brookins\n|Spencer Paige\n|Alex Caceres\n|Kyle Watson\n|Cody McKenzie\n|Dane Sayers\n|}\n* Caceres explains how his love of [[Bruce Lee]] led to him pursuing a career in martial arts.\n* St-Pierre picked Caceres to face Lentz, who trained under [[Kurt Pellegrino]].\n* '''Alex Caceres''' defeated Jeff Lentz by submission (triangle choke) at 2:27 of the second round.\n\n'''Episode 3: Unleash Hell'''\n* Alex Caceres celebrated his victory by getting drunk, but his attitude when drunk was mocked.\n* Olympic silver medalist wrestler [[Gia Sissaouri]] trained with the red team.\n* St-Pierre picked Johnson to face Wilkinson.\n* Former heavyweight boxing champion [[Mike Tyson]] visited ''The Ultimate Fighter'' gym, to see Team GSP.\n* '''Michael Johnson''' defeated Aaron Wilkinson by submission (rear naked choke) at 0:37 of the third round.\n* Tyson later claimed that Johnson's speed won the fight, as Wilkinson was the better boxer. Koscheck also claimed that the fight should not have gone to third round, as he believed that Wilkinson had done enough to win the first two.\n\n'''Episode 4: Love to Hate'''\n* Tyson talked with Team GSP about his attitude toward fighting and how to win.\n* Sevak Magakian and Alex Caceres got into a heated argument and nearly fought at the fighter house. However, they were separated by their fellow fighters before any punches were thrown.\n* St-Pierre announced the next fight: Watson against Main\n* '''Kyle Watson''' defeated Andy Main by submission with a rear-naked choke in the second round.\n\n'''Episode 5: Disrespectful'''\n* Jon Fitch arrived at the training center to train with the yellow team.\n* [[Jean-Charles Skarbowsky]] also arrived, intoxicated and following a 14-hour flight from Paris, to train Muay Thai with Team GSP.\n* [[Chuck Liddell]] also arrived and shared some words of wisdom with Team Koscheck\n* Alex Caceres annoyed teammate Michael Johnson by putting bleach into Nam Phan's fabric conditioner – the very same used by Johnson.\n* St-Pierre matched Paige against Phan.\n* '''Nam Phan''' defeated Spencer Paige by decision after two rounds.\n* Paige suffered a broken hand in the fight.\n\n'''Episode 6: Win or Die'''\n* Koscheck annoyed Michael Johnson by calling him out-of-shape and interrupting him during stretches. The yellow team further compounded frustration after celebrating loudly.\n* McKenzie continued to joke around with Koscheck, which annoyed him.\n* Koscheck announced the next fight Stevens against McKenzie\n* '''Cody McKenzie''' defeated Marc Stevens by technical submission (guillotine choke) at 0:17 of the first round.\n* St-Pierre matched Brookins against Magakian\n* '''Jonathan Brookins''' defeated Sevak Magakian via submission (rear naked choke) at 2:04 of the first round.\n\n'''Episode 7: Second Chance'''\n* Koscheck defeated St-Pierre in a baseball home run derby to win the Coaches' Challenge.\n* '''Sako Chivitchian''' defeated Dane Sayers by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n* The wildcards were announced as Aaron Wilkinson and Marc Stevens.\n\n'''Episode 8: Kos in a Commotion'''\n* Cleopas Ncube is introduced by St-Pierre.\n* Koscheck got into a physical confrontation with Team GSP paramedic Brad Tate.\n* Wilkinson received a phone call from his mother, who informed him his grandmother had died.\n* '''Aaron Wilkinson''' defeated Marc Stevens by submission (guillotine choke) at 0:47 of the second round.\n* White and the coaches called in each fighter to ask which opponent they preferred to face. Every fighter stated their interest in facing Caceres.\n* The quarterfinal fights were announced:\n:*Jonathan Brookins vs. Sako Chivitchian\n:*Cody McKenzie vs Nam Phan\n:*Kyle Watson vs. Aaron Wilkinson\n:*Michael Johnson vs. Alex Caceres\n\n'''Episode 9: Personal'''\n* '''Jonathan Brookins''' defeated Sako Chivitchian by submission (rear naked choke) at 2:05 of the first round.\n* '''Nam Phan''' defeated Cody McKenzie by TKO (punches) at 2:54 of the second round.\n* After Phan's win, Koscheck stormed the Octagon, screaming and yelling to celebrate the win.\n* St-Pierre notes that Koscheck is blatantly and obnoxiously celebrating to get under his skin. He admits that Koscheck got under his skin and says it will motivate him that much more in preparing for the coaches' fight.\n\n'''Episode 10: Spinning Tornado'''\n* '''Kyle Watson''' defeated Aaron Wilkinson by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:59 in the first round.\n* '''Michael Johnson''' defeated Alex Caceres by unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n* White and the coaches unanimously agreed on the matches for the semifinals:\n:*Jonathan Brookins vs. Kyle Watson\n:*Michael Johnson vs Nam Phan\n\n'''Episode 11: Victory's All That Matters'''\n* '''Jonathan Brookins''' defeated Kyle Watson by unanimous decision (30–27, 30–27, 30–27) after three rounds.\n* '''Michael Johnson''' defeated Nam Phan by split decision (29–28, 28–29, 29–28) after three rounds.\n* There was controversy over this fight as both St-Pierre and Koscheck argued that his fighter won.\n* Brookins and Johnson, both of Team GSP, are announced as the season's finalists.\n\n==Tournament bracket==\n{{Round16\n\n\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} Spencer Paige|2|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Nam Phan'''|'''UD'''\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} $380,025\n|previousevent = [[UFC 123|UFC 123: Rampage vs. Machida]]\n|followingevent = [[UFC 124|UFC 124: St-Pierre vs. Koscheck 2]]\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team GSP vs. Team Koscheck Finale''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 12 Finale''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[sporting event|event]] held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] at the [[Palms Casino Resort]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]] on December 4, 2010.{{cite web |url=https://www.ufc.com/event/TUF12-Finale#/fight|title=The Ultimate Fighter: Team GSP vs. Team Koscheck Finale|publisher=www.ufc.com}}\n\n===Background===\nThis was the first event in the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] to integrate the featherweight & bantamweight classes from [[World Extreme Cagefighting]], which folded later that month. It was also the first event to feature a then-contracted WEC lightweight fighter ([[Pablo Garza (fighter)|Pablo Garza]]) in a UFC contest, though he debuted in the UFC at featherweight.\n\n[[Leonard Garcia]] was set to fight [[Tyler Toner]] at this event, but withdrew from the contest, with Team Quest fighter & IFL veteran, [[Ian Loveland]] stepping in as Garcia's replacement.{{cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Garcia-Out-Loveland-In-Against-Toner-at-TUF-12-Finale-28388|title=Garcia Out, Loveland In Against Toner at TUF 12 Finale|author=Jordan Breen}} It was later reported that Garcia withdrew to face [[Nam Phan]],{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21584/leonard-garcia-vs-nam-phan-slotted-for-the-ultimate-fighter-12-finale.mma|title=Leonard Garcia vs. Nam Phan slotted for The Ultimate Fighter 12 Finale|author=Dann Stupp|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101204000434/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21584/leonard-garcia-vs-nam-phan-slotted-for-the-ultimate-fighter-12-finale.mma|archive-date=2010-12-04}} after his original opponent [[Alex Caceres]] was forced from the bout due to injury.{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/blog/cagewriter/post/-Bruce-Leeroy-absence-makes-Garcia-Phan-possibl?urn=mma-291282|title='Bruce Leeroy' absence makes Garcia-Phan possible at TUF 12 Finale|author=Steve Cofield}}\n\n[[Nick Pace]] missed weight for his bantamweight bout against [[Will Campuzano]], missing the 135 mark by three pounds. The fight was changed to a catchweight bout and Pace was fined 20% of his purse.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21601/the-ultimate-fighter-12-finale-live-weigh-in-results.mma|title=The Ultimate Fighter 12 Finale weigh-in results: Brookins, Johnson official|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101206011316/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21601/the-ultimate-fighter-12-finale-live-weigh-in-results.mma|archive-date=2010-12-06}}\n\n==Results==\n\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Jonathan Brookins]]\n|def.\n|[[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 29–28, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|TUF 12 Lightweight Tournament Final\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Stephan Bonnar]]\n|def.\n|[[Igor Pokrajac]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–26, 29–26, 29–26)\n|3\n|5:00\n|Pokrajac had 1 point deducted at the end of round 2 for two knee strikes on a downed opponent. [[UFC 121|UFC 121: Lesnar vs. Velasquez]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC 123|UFC 123: Rampage vs. Machida]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 122: Marquardt vs. Okami''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on November 13, 2010, at [[König Pilsener Arena]] in [[Oberhausen, Germany]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC122#/fight |title=UFC 122: Marquardt vs. Okami|publisher=ufc.com |date=September 20, 2010}} This event aired on the same day, via [[Broadcast delay|tape delay]], on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike TV]] in the [[United States|U.S]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20149/spike-tv-to-air-ufc-122-via-same-day-delay-from-germany.mma|title=Spike TV to air UFC 122 via same-day delay from Germany|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=August 3, 2010|access-date=August 3, 2010|archive-date=May 25, 2012|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525132319/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20149/spike-tv-to-air-ufc-122-via-same-day-delay-from-germany.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\nThis was the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]]'s second event in [[Germany]] and second in continental [[Europe]]. The sport has been met with severe criticism by German media, which led to the banning of minors from the first German [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event ([[UFC 99]]).{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15282/ufc-rep-ufc-returning-to-germany-in-2010.mma|title=UFC rep: UFC returning to Germany in 2010|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=June 22, 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120715215336/http://mmajunkie.com/news/15282/ufc-rep-ufc-returning-to-germany-in-2010.mma|archive-date=July 15, 2012}}\n\nThe sport itself was actually banned from broadcast altogether in Germany in March 2010 due to the \"extent of violence [being] shown to be unacceptable.\"{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Update-UFC-Banned-from-German-TV-23317|title=UFC Banned from German TV |last=Leidecker |first=Tim |work=[[Sherdog]] |date=March 19, 2010 |access-date=November 22, 2010}}{{cite web|url=http://www.dailytech.com/Switzerland+Bans+Violent+Video+Games+Germany+Bans+MMA+on+TV/article17949.htm|title=Switzerland bans violent video games; Germany bans MMA on TV|publisher=dailytech.com|date=March 22, 2010|access-date=July 12, 2010|archive-date=July 23, 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100723022014/http://www.dailytech.com/Switzerland+Bans+Violent+Video+Games+Germany+Bans+MMA+on+TV/article17949.htm|url-status=dead}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11661&zoneid=13|title=UFC plans return trip to Germany|publisher=mmaweekly.com|date=June 3, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100605013937/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=11661&zoneid=13|archive-date=June 5, 2010}} Regardless, UFC combated the opposition, after stating: \"Not getting TV isn't going to stop us; we're going to keep going. We can't get into Ontario; we're opening an office there. It doesn't bother us.\"\n\n[[Vitor Belfort]] was set to face [[Yushin Okami]] in the main event, with the winner receiving a [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] title shot. However, on September 21, 2010, Belfort withdrew from the fight to face [[Anderson Silva]]. [[Nate Marquardt]] ended up fighting Yushin Okami in the main event, which would still determine the next #1 contender.[http://www.ufc.com/news/spike-122-release-102610 UFC 122 to Determine Title Shot]\n\n[[Vladimir Matyushenko]] was scheduled to face [[Jason Brilz]] at this event, but Brilz was forced off the card on October 6 with a back injury and replaced by [[Alexandre Ferreira (fighter)|Alexandre Ferreira]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/20913/injury-forces-brilz-out-of-ufc-122-matyushenko-expected-to-meet-ferreira.mma|title=Injury forces Brilz out of UFC 122, Matyushenko expected to meet Ferreira|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=October 6, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120711005655/http://mmajunkie.com/news/20913/injury-forces-brilz-out-of-ufc-122-matyushenko-expected-to-meet-ferreira.mma|archive-date=July 11, 2012}} The Matyushenko/Brilz matchup was rescheduled for [[UFC 129]] in April 2011.\n\n[[Pascal Krauss]] was expected to make his promotional debut against fellow newcomer [[Kenny Robertson]],{{cite [[Auburn Hills, Michigan]].{{Cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC123#/fight|title=UFC 123: Rampage vs. Machida|publisher=ufc.com|date=September 14, 2010}} It was the first [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event in the [[Metro Detroit]] area since ''[[UFC 9]]''.\n\n==Background==\n\nA private ceremony was hosted an hour before the start of the card, where [[José Aldo]] was awarded the inaugural [[UFC Featherweight Championship]] belt by [[Dana White]]. Fans in the building were not permitted to view the ceremony.http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/11/20/1827175/jose-aldo-presented-with-ufc-featherweight-belt-at-ufc-123 {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101124174754/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/11/20/1827175/jose-aldo-presented-with-ufc-featherweight-belt-at-ufc-123 |date=2010-11-24 }} Jose Aldo Presented With UFC Featherweight Belt at UFC 123\n\n''UFC 123'' featured preliminary fights live on [[Spike (TV network)|Spike TV]].\n\nOn October 13, [[Rory MacDonald (fighter)|Rory MacDonald]] had to withdraw from his fight against [[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]]. O'Brien replaced Ruediger.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21216/with-gabe-ruediger-out-paul-kelly-vs-t-j-obrien-official-for-ufc-123.mma|title=With Gabe Ruediger out, Paul Kelly vs. T.J. Penn'''\n*'''Submission of the Night: Phil Davis'''\n\n==Controversy==\nA formal complaint was filed with Michigan's Unarmed Combat Commission regarding the timekeeping of the Harris-Falcão match. Near the end of the first round, Falcão was choking Harris. The allegation is that the timekeeper ended the round six seconds early and in turn had the round not ended early, Falcão may have successfully choked out Harris. Despite Falcão ultimately winning the fight by decision, the formal complaint was filed on January 13, 2011.{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=6058034|title=Complaint alleges round ended early|first=Josh |last=Gross|work=[[ESPN.com]] |date=January 25, 2011 |access-date=August 16, 2012}}\n\n==See also==\n* [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n* [[List of UFC champions]]\n* and the fifth event held in [[Canada]] along with [[UFC 115]] which was held in [[Vancouver]], [[British Columbia]].\n\n==Background==\n\nA training injury forced [[Jason MacDonald]] out of his fight with [[Rafael Natal]] on October 20, 2010. He was replaced by UFC newcomer Jesse Bongfeldt.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21083/jason-macdonald-out-rafael-natal-faces-newcomer-jesse-bongfeldt-at-ufc-124.mma|title=Jason MacDonald out, Rafael Natal faces newcomer Jesse Bongfeldt at UFC 124|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=October 20, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101023084416/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21083/jason-macdonald-out-rafael-natal-faces-newcomer-jesse-bongfeldt-at-ufc-124.mma|archive-date=October 23, 2010}}\n\nOn October 26, 2010, [[Anthony Waldburger]] had to pull out of his fight with [[Matthew Riddle]] due to an injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21159/injured-anthony-t-j-waldburger-out-of-ufc-124-fight-with-matt-riddle.mma|title=Injured Anthony \"T.J.\" Waldburger out of UFC 124 fight with Matt Riddle|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=October 26, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130128230550/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2010/10/injured-anthony-t-j-waldburger-out-of-ufc-124-fight-with-matt-riddle|archive-date=January 28, 2013}} He was replaced by [[Sean Pierson]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21170/sean-pierson-replaces-injured-t-j-waldburger-meets-matt-riddle-at-ufc-124.mma|title=Sean Pierson replaces injured T.J. [[Dana White]] stated after that he would never do this again and said \"The real fight of the night belonged to Matt Riddle and Sean Pierson\".\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Georges St-Pierre]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Josh Koscheck]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (50–45, 50–45, 50–45)\n|5\n|5:00\n|For the [[UFC Welterweight Championship]]\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Stefan Struve]]\n|def.\n|[[Sean McCorkle]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|1\n|3:55\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]]\n|def.\n|[[Charles Oliveira]]\n|Submission (kneebar)\n|1\n|1:59\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Mac Danzig]]\n|def.\n|[[Joe Stevenson]]\n|KO (punch)\n|1\n|1:54\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]]\n|def.\n|[[John Howard (fighter)|John Howard]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 30–27, 30–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card (UFC.com live stream)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]]\n|def.\n|[[Joe Doerksen]]\n|Decision (split) (29–28, 28–29, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Mark Bocek]]\n|def.\n|[[Dustin Hazelett]]\n|Submission (triangle armbar)\n|1\n|2:33\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Rafael Natal]]\n|vs.\n|Jesse Bongfeldt\n|Draw (majority) (28–28, 28–28, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Sean Pierson]]\n|def.\n|[[Matthew Riddle]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 30–27, 30–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Ricardo Almeida]]\n|def.\n|[[T. Resolution|publisher=ufc.com|date=October 21, 2010}}\n\n==Background==\nThe planned rematch between [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Anderson Silva]] and [[Chael Sonnen]] from their [[UFC 117]] bout was at one point thought to take place at this event.{{cite web | url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2010/09/07/chael-sonnen-vs-anderson-silva-ii-possible-for-new-year%E2%80%99s-day-event/ | title=MMA Archives }}{{cite web | url=http://mmafrenzy.com/15857/anderson-silva-vs-chael-sonnen-frankie-edgar-vs-gray-maynard-possible-for-ufc-125/ | title=MMA Archives }}\n\nOn October 26, 2010, [[Shane Carwin]] had to pull out of his fight with [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]] due to a back injury that required surgery. Nelson was then pulled from the card altogether.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21157/injured-shane-carwin-pulls-out-of-ufc-125-co-main-event-with-roy-nelson.mma|title=Injured Shane Carwin pulls out of UFC 125 co-main event with Roy Nelson|url-status=dead|archiveurl=https://archive.today/20130129010828/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2010/10/injured-shane-carwin-pulls-out-of-ufc-125-co-main-event-with-roy-nelson|archivedate=2013-01-29}}\n\nOn October 28, 2010, [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] president [[Dana White]] announced that [[World Extreme Cagefighting]] (WEC) would merge with the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] and that [[List of WEC champions#Featherweight Championship|WEC Featherweight Champion]] [[José Aldo]] would be promoted to [[List of UFC champions#Featherweight Championship|UFC Featherweight Champion]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21190/new-ufc-featherweight-champ-jose-aldo-defends-title-at-ufc-125.mma|title=New UFC Featherweight champ Jose Aldo defends title at UFC 125|url-status=dead|archiveurl=https://archive.today/20130129001131/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2010/10/new-ufc-featherweight-champ-jose-aldo-defends-title-at-ufc-125|archivedate=2013-01-29}} [[Josh Grispi]] was pulled from a planned [[WEC 52]] bout with [[Erik Koch]] to challenge Aldo for the [[Featherweight (MMA)|Featherweight]] title at this event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2010/10/29/1782290/ufc-125-jose-aldo-vs-josh-grispi-in-the-works-for-jan-1-in-las-vegas|title=UFC 125: Jose Aldo vs Josh Grispi in the works for Jan. 1 in Las Vegas|author=Fernando Quiles Jr.|date=29 October 2010}} However, Aldo was later forced off the card with an injury.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21510/new-ufc-champ-jose-aldo-forced-to-withdraw-from-ufc-125.mma|title=Injury forces new UFC champ Jose Aldo to withdraw from UFC 125|publisher=MMAjunkie|url-status=dead|archiveurl=https://archive.today/20120711003441/http://mmajunkie.com/news/21510/new-ufc-champ-jose-aldo-forced-to-withdraw-from-ufc-125.mma|archivedate=2012-07-11}} Grispi remained on the card and fought [[Dustin Poirier]].{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/21528/with-jose-aldo-out-dustin-poirier-faces-josh-grispi-at-ufc-125.mma|title=With Jose Aldo out, [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] bout between [[Cole Miller]] and [[Matt Wiman]], which was originally scheduled to take place at this event, was moved to [[UFC: Fight For The Troops 2]].{{cite
